## 0. Work queue (consolidated 2026-07-10; 2-pass-audited same day)

### ⚠️ How to enumerate the queue in a fresh session — READ FIRST

**A top-down read of the §0 tables alone is NOT sufficient and has already produced a wrong
"what's left" answer** (2026-07-25: the five blocked tunes, T9, F-OPT and F-SYNC were all reported
as "queue empty except D3" because none of them had a §0 row — they existed only as prose bullets
or in a sibling doc). Open items live in **six** places. Read all six, in this order:

| # | location | what only lives here | how to find it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **§0 / §0a tables** (this file) | programs, audit rows, weekly bug-queue mirror | `grep -n "\| TODO\|\| OWNER\|IN-PROGRESS\|BUILT-HOLD" ` on this file |
| 2 | **§4b chip register** (this file) | one-off findings parked as background tasks | `awk -F'\|' 'NF>3 && $5 ~ /^ *(\*\*)?(OPEN\|PARTIAL\|RESEARCHED)/ {print $2" => "substr($5,1,60)}'` — anchor on the status cell's **first word**: the DONE cells are long narratives that mention "OPEN" mid-sentence, so a plain `/OPEN/` returns false positives, and **a status cell must therefore LEAD with its verdict**. ⚠️ **Escaped-pipe trap (found by E2E-01 T4, 2026-07-31): a cell containing `\|` (e.g. task_f10a03's `(version\|instrument)`, G15's `\|close−open\|`) shifts the status out of field 5 — the awk MISSES a genuinely OPEN chip and can read mid-cell garbage as status.** Cross-check with a last-cell anchor (`grep -n '\| OPEN \|$'` on this file) before trusting the awk's list |
| 3 | **§9 architecture candidates** (this file) | Ousterhout deepening rows 02–06 | read §9a/§9b directly; they carry **no status column** — see the §9 rule below, a script that reads the last cell here reports DONE rows as open |
| 4 | **prose bullets inside dated UPDATE blocks** (this file) | items declared mid-narrative and never promoted to a row | `grep -n "OPEN CHIPS\|new ledger item\|stays open\|not yet built"` |
| 5 | **the newest `docs/signal-analysis/` tune table** | the whole **T-namespace** (T1…T23) — regenerated each session, no durable register of its own | `grep -n "^\| T[0-9]" docs/signal-analysis/$(ls docs/signal-analysis \| grep session-findings \| tail -1)` then keep rows whose status is `PROPOSED` |
| 6 | **the current weekly bug queue** (`docs/signal-analysis/YYYY-MM-DD-weekly-bug-queue.md`) | B/S/D rows + the "blocked tunes" and "parked proposal" bullets under the owner-decision pack | read the whole file — the closing bullets carry items the table does not |

**Single-status rule.** An item's status is authoritative in **this file's** row and nowhere else.
Source docs (bug queues, session findings, audits) carry evidence and may show a status for local
readability, but **when they disagree, the ledger row wins** — and the drifted one gets corrected in
the same PR. Two live examples of the drift this rule exists to stop: `task_a2ae20ed` read OPEN here
while its own sweep doc said CLOSED; bug-queue rows B1–B4 read `IN PROGRESS`/`queued` for a day after
all four had merged AND deployed.

**Promotion rule.** Anything declared open in a prose bullet, a tune table, or a sibling doc **must
get a §0 row in the same PR that declares it.** A bullet is not a queue entry — nobody finds it.
⚠️ **Corollary the promotion rule does NOT cover, and it has bitten: promoting an item does not
retire the prose that spawned it.** The paragraph keeps its open-item wording forever, because nobody
goes back to edit narrative once a row exists. **When an item is promoted, leave a `→ row Gn` pointer
on the prose in the SAME edit** (see the F-OPT/F-SYNC paragraphs in §0's 2026-07-20 block for the
shape). Before briefing anything sourced from prose, grep the item name for a promoted row.

**⚠️ §9 rows carry NO status column — strikethrough on the id is the DONE marker.** Their columns are
`# | deepening | badge | files | gate`, so the LAST cell is *"how you would take it"* and legitimately
reads e.g. `**HOLD-tier parity firewall …**` on a row that shipped weeks ago. The status lives (a) as
`~~**04**~~` strikethrough on the id and (b) inside the *deepening* cell, which leads with `DONE`.
**A struck-through id means DONE in every table in this file and overrides every other signal.**

⚠️ **COST RECORD 2026-08-02 — one enumeration pass produced FOUR false dispatches, and the ledger was
only half the cause.** Two (F-OPT, F-SYNC) came from the prose corollary above. Two (§9-02, §9-04)
came from a parser that read §9's last cell as a status and saw `HOLD-tier …` — both rows were struck
through and said `DONE 2026-07-29` in cell 2. Every builder caught its own premise before writing
code, which is the only reason this cost minutes. **Two takeaways: a scripted enumeration must
implement the strikethrough rule and the six locations, not just grep for status words; and every
brief must carry a STEP 0 telling the builder to verify the premise against the code first.**

- ✅ **FIXED [#901](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/901) (`2b5b74a2`) — main ci-java GREEN again.** `SignalEngineIntegrationTest.everyEvaluationIsCountedExactlyOnceSoTheOutcomeSumIsTheEvaluationCount` (task_37ee83e0) was RED ON MAIN from #895's merge (`17a69ea2`) — a **test-harness delivery race, not an engine bug** (NOT caused by #900; proven red on the #895 merge commit itself). The cold `SIGSUM<rand>` symbol's per-token channel is subscribed only after `loadedSlugs()` flips (`SignalEngine.java:680`) and `resubscribe()` (`:685`) rebuilds the Redis listener with an async SUBSCRIBE, so bars published into the gap drop on a 2-core runner. A "prove the subscription is live" probe is structurally unfixable (loadedSlugs flips BEFORE resubscribe; single + two-probe both stayed red on CI). Fix (test-only): **publish each bar and confirm its exact +2 landed before the next** — rides out the subscribe gap + any mid-publish rebuild, keeps the exact-count invariant strong. Cross-vendor Codex took 3 rounds (caught the outgoing-listener race, then a weakened exact-count invariant). **Lesson: the red hid a full day behind GREEN PR checks — only the `push:main` ci-java run went red; check `gh run list --workflow ci-java.yml --branch main` after a merge.**
- **PENDING:** one batched `frontend-react` rebuild+redeploy for #897+#899 (market closed → low urgency; #900 is CI/spec-gen only, no deploy).

`permission denied for schema marketdata` (`admin/V001:18`). The doc carries a STOP block. ~~§1/§2 (the 9-of-9

**⚠️ UPDATE 2026-07-17 — §1 IS NOW INVALIDATED TOO. THE DETECTOR'S CORE PREMISE IS DEAD ON A SECOND,
INDEPENDENT AXIS. Read this before reviving F10 Part B.** "Bars present + rejections absent ⇒ the engine is
broken" is **FALSE**: it would **false-positive on every bearish leg**. Proven by two independent investigations
(Opus + Fable, separate models, no shared framing) plus a live thread dump, after the Architect escalated an
84-minute silence as a live starvation — **and was wrong; the engine was healthy the whole time.**
**MECHANISM:** `SignalEngine.evaluateAtBarClose:1003-1013` has **no else-branch** — both `recordRejection` call
sites sit INSIDE `evaluation.isPresent() && evaluation.get().entry()`, so `strategy.signal_rejections` records
**only CONFLUENCE-gate blocks on bars whose CHART gate already passed**. A chart-stage no-entry writes nothing.
Every published scalper shares the same two required scorers on one 3m NIFTY-future series (`rsi14`/`rsi_momentum`
w=1.0 + `supertrend`/`direction` w=1.0, `threshold: 0.2`), so composite = (s_rsi + s_st)/2 ⇒ **SuperTrend DOWN +
RSI < 58 ⇒ composite < 0.2 ⇒ EVERY scalper silent simultaneously**. **The "9 rejections per 3m" cadence is NOT a
health baseline — it is a supertrend-UP indicator.** Both agents reconstructed ST(10,2)+RSI(14) from live data and
predicted **every gap edge bar-exactly on two separate days**, including an isolated single-bar hole no stall theory
can produce; Fable additionally matched a **partial** batch gate-by-gate (the 5 CE gated `close > vwma20` silent,
the 4 vwap-only CE wrote, the 2 PE gated `close < vwma20` wrote) and observed that `scalp-trending-oi-nifty`
(`close > vwma20`) **and its PE twin** (`close < vwma20`) were BOTH silent for 80 min — exactly one gate can be true
per bar, so only the shared composite can silence both. **A thread dump taken DURING a blackout shows `signal-eval`
parked on `LinkedBlockingQueue.take()` — its own executor queue, `cpu=1724ms / elapsed=6574s` — an IDLE worker, not
a stall.** So §1's "zero/truncated rejections on 9 of 9 trading days" is plausibly **measuring market direction, not
engine health** (that figure was never re-derived — it is `recalled`). What survives: **every existing detector is
still blind** (§2), and the silent no-entry path was a REAL observability defect — 4 states (engine dead / chart-gate
closed / indicators warming / composite below threshold) shared ONE DB signature. **FIXED + DEPLOYED LIVE:
[#895](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/895) @ 17a69ea2** — `ay_signal_eval_outcome_total{outcome}`,
7 tags, **verified live pre-registered at 0.0 on a fresh boot** (a lazily-created counter would leave a MISSING `fired`
series indistinguishable from a dead engine — the same ambiguity, one level up). Built **structurally**: `decideEntry`
now RETURNS an `Outcome` and `evaluateAtBarClose` holds the ONLY increment site, so **a future silent `return;` is a
COMPILE ERROR, not a blind spot**. The `Σ(outcomes) == evaluations` invariant is mutation-proven BOTH ways (undercount
34 RED, double-count 80 RED, real 40 GREEN) — **`>= 40` would have gone green at 80**, hence an exact assertion.
⚠️ **RETRACTED 2026-07-18 — an earlier version of THIS BLOCK claimed #895 "gives a future F10 Part B revival a sound
liveness primitive it lacked". THAT CLAIM IS FALSE and was the Architect's, written the same day, unverified.**
`ay_signal_eval_outcome_total`'s increment site sits INSIDE the `else` of `if (activeEntry.isPresent())`
(`SignalEngine:1063`, counter at `:1105-1113`), BELOW two `continue`s — `!inUniverse` (`:1017`) and
`!withinSessionWindow` (`:1020-1022`) — and btst is never evaluated from `onClosedBar` at all (`:1026-1029`).
**So Σ(outcomes) is LEGITIMATELY FLAT while bars flow** in four normal states: outside a session window, whole book in
position, context-only symbols, btst. **Measured live: 51 of the intraday strategies close their window at
15:00/12:00/14:30 while the watchdog arms 09:20→15:30 (`SubscriberHealthCanary:72-73`) — so Σ(outcomes) is
STRUCTURALLY FLAT 15:00–15:30 EVERY non-expiry day.** A "bars arriving + Σ flat ⇒ alarm" detector **pages every
afternoon**. It is **the same mistake as the dead design, one layer up**: rejections were *direction*-dependent;
Σ(outcomes) is *window*- and *position*-dependent. **#895 is an ATTRIBUTION primitive — an evidence panel. NEVER arm
anything on it.**
**THE ONLY signals safe to key liveness on** are `lastBarReceivedAtMs` (`SignalEngine:361`, stamped at `:954` as the
FIRST line of `onCandleMessage` — before ANY universe/window/position/loaded logic) and `lastBarEvaluatedAtMs`. Both
are direction-, window- AND position-independent. Rejections / `signals` / gate output / Σ(outcomes) are all confounded.
**PART B IS ALREADY SHIPPED — IT IS #886, AND THE OWNER'S FORK IS MOOT.** Post-#886 `SubscriberHealthCanary`: gate 3 is
`registry.countEnabledPublished() == 0` (`:125`; live = 69) so `loaded == 0` no longer disarms it (pinned by
`SubscriberHealthCanaryTest:119`); gate 4 is `feedAge != Long.MAX_VALUE && feedAge > feedFreshMs` (`:172`) so
"can't tell" is suspicious not healthy; knobs wired (`application.yml:63-66`, `docker-compose.yml:393-395`).
**F10 replay against the live code: boot ~08:45 → `loaded=0` → no subscriptions → no bars; `lastBarReceivedAtMs` seeded
at boot (`:424`); at the 09:20 sweep `receiveGap ≈ 35min ≫ 180s`, `evalLag ≈ 0`, feed fresh → IT FIRES.** The
cold-start is detected today. **And the market-data-contract-vs-cross-schema-grant fork is moot on BOTH prongs** — the
engine already holds `lastBarReceivedAtMs`, a strictly BETTER oracle than either: a market-data contract answers "did
market-data WRITE bars", whose YES does not imply the engine RECEIVED them (the 07-07 subscription drop is exactly that
gap), and the grant punctures D10 for a worse answer. **Build neither.**
**⚠️ ~~DO NOT ARM chip task_a6c12601 / #868 `SignalStarvationCanary` — it is a LIVE LANDMINE (→ chip task_3a928626,
retire the class).~~ RESOLVED 2026-07-26 — THE CLASS IS RETIRED, not left dormant** (see the task_3a928626 row in §4b).
The reasoning below is kept because it is the reusable lesson, not because the class still exists.** It keys on `lastGateOutputAtMs`, written only PAST the chart gate, so on a SuperTrend-DOWN leg
`outputGap > windowMs` while its guards (`receiveGap < barGapMs`, `evalLag < barGapMs`) are BOTH satisfied ⇒ **it
pages**. **The 2026-07-17 false alarm was this canary's exact signature, executed by hand.** Arming it automates that
mistake daily.
**The one GENUINE gap:** `SwingBatchHeartbeat` (`:36`, cron `0 15 20 * * MON-FRI` at `:51`) pings ONCE at 20:15 IST and
proves only that the swing BATCH ran — **a stack dead 09:15–15:30 that recovers by 20:15 pings happily while the whole
session is lost.** A session-cadence heartbeat (~10min, 09:30–15:20 IST, gated ONLY on `receiveGap < barGapMs`, never on
outcomes) on the existing external dead-man's-switch closes it; absence-is-the-alarm survives process death and cannot
be muted in-process. Owner-gated on creating the external check.
**BUILT + MERGED 2026-07-19 — [#941](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/941) @ f1b412f6 (`SessionLivenessHeartbeat`, strategy-signal; clean-tier, default-OFF, UNDEPLOYED — dormant, rides the next deploy).** The IN-STACK half is closed: `@Scheduled(0 */10 9-15, Asia/Kolkata)` pings a SEPARATE external monitor every ~10min within [09:30,15:20) IST, gated ONLY on `SignalEngine.lastBarReceivedAtMs()` receive-gap (< `bar-gap-ms`) + calendar-open — never rejections/gate-output/outcomes (the SignalStarvationCanary false-page class). Withholds the ping when bars aren't arriving (absence-is-the-alarm). Default pool, NOT `monitorTaskScheduler` (a 5s HTTP ping there would starve `SubscriberHealthCanary.sweep`). Dormant via `@ConditionalOnProperty(artha.heartbeat.session-url)` + `artha.signals.engine-enabled` (codex cross-vendor caught the missing engine-lifecycle condition = an engine-off boot break the builder+Architect both missed). **OWNER STEP TO ARM:** create the external check (healthchecks.io/UptimeRobot) expecting a ~10min ping in-window, set `ARTHA_HEARTBEAT_SESSION_URL` in `.env`, redeploy. ⚠️ arming caveat: a weekday NSE holiday withholds ALL pings (correct — no session) → pause the check on NSE holidays or use a generous grace, else a holiday false-alarm.
**HIGHEST-VALUE NEXT ACTION IS A DRILL, NOT A BUILD:** #886's receive-gap path is drill-proven on COLD-START ONLY; a
mid-session subscription drop is reconstructed, not observed. If a drill shows it not firing there, the eval/receive
composition needs rework — and that would overturn "Part B is already shipped". **Re-read §3.1 regardless: REST and JDBC
both remain blind.** Also found: **the PE variants score a LONG-BIASED composite** (`direction` = bullish→1) so
they can essentially only fire when their own thesis is wrong — owner-gated, chip **task_79092520**. Chip
task_87a94cb2 was filed then WITHDRAWN — its premise (a 3m partial-bucket mismatch) was refuted: `PartialBucketCanary`
is *purely observational* and its 0.7% skew is the benign tick-agg-vs-persisted noise its own javadoc anticipates,
not the ~67% a frozen first-minute partial gives.

Historical detail below. ⛔ **DEFERRED 2026-07-26, NOT SHIPPABLE: 6 Critical money defects + 2 Major regressions** found by a fresh cross-vendor review on the CURRENT base. Rebase + renumber are DONE and pushed as **`origin/feat/swing-batch-catchup`** (use this, not the old `worktree-agent-abb02bf43adbb895d`): zero-conflict rebase onto main, `V046__swing_catchup_runs`→**`V047`** (main's V046__engine_reloads is applied live; lineage V044..V047 contiguous), verify green **1019 tests**. THE SIX CRITICALS: (1) the sweep CONTINUES past a partial session — Thursday's bar missing + Friday's present ⇒ Friday closes the position at Friday's price and Thursday's retry then sees no anchor and falsely completes; (2) historical runs evaluate CURRENTLY-active lots without filtering as-of the pinned bar date, so a Friday anchor settles against Thursday's truncated bar and Manas can close an averaged position containing FUTURE lots; (3) durable `PENDING` rows fall out of the `maxAttempts+2` window forever — a 7-session outage leaves the oldest session never reaching DONE or ABANDONED; (4) the claim is NOT atomic with the money effect — paper failures are swallowed in the listeners while the sweep still declares DONE, leaving an active signal with no paper open or a position with no anchor; (5) `DISARMED` records CURRENT intent, so re-enabling replays a deliberately-skipped session and disabling suppresses an armed-but-missed one; (6) readiness and candidates come from two SEPARATE HTTP fetches, so it can validate session X and trade session Y's names. MAJORS: the unqualified `@Scheduled` shares the default pool with the 15s SL/TP sweep, funnel calls have no timeout and the market-hours check is START-ONLY (the Architect reached this one independently); and the manual-close path now runs full `positionDetail()` enrichment BEFORE closing, so an emergency explicit-price close can hang. ⚠️ **Findings 1/2/3/5 look BASE-INDEPENDENT — i.e. missed by the three cross-vendor rounds this branch passed in July, not introduced by the rebase. Treat any revived long-parked branch as unreviewed.** Owner decision: fix the six, or solve the missed-batch incident another way. ⚠️ **MIGRATION NUMBER NOW COLLIDES (verified 2026-07-25):** the branch carries `V046__swing_catchup_runs.sql`, but main shipped `V046__engine_reloads.sql` and it is APPLIED LIVE (`strategy.flyway_schema_history`, 2026-07-25 09:18 UTC). - **Catch-up auto-replay ([#1036](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1036) `2e4ea6f0`, armed 2026-07-27 ~22:45 IST) — ✅ FIRST ARMED SWEEP VERIFIED 2026-07-28 08:35 IST, fail-closed guard proven on a real session, ZERO money effect.** Full writeup: [`docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-28-swing-catchup-first-armed-sweep.md`](../../signal-analysis/2026-07-28-swing-catchup-first-armed-sweep.md). **It was NOT the predicted clean no-op**: the sweep (`swing-catchup-sched-1`, 08:34:59 IST) found `2026-07-17` — the original outage session, a genuine hole in `swing_batch_runs` between 07-16 and 07-20 — inside its 7-session window (`max-attempts + 2`, oldest slot) and **REFUSED to replay it**, writing two terminal `ABANDONED / NO_SCHEDULE_INTENT` rows (`attempts = 0`, so the atomic claim was never taken) and logging at ERROR. Correct by design: 07-17 predates the intent ledger (`swing_batch_schedule_intents` `count=2, min=max=2026-07-27`), so schedule-time arming is unknowable and the code refuses rather than inferring today's flag — exactly the round-5 Critical's requirement. **Money safety: paper positions opened 0 / closed 0, orders 0, paper_events 0 over the 12 h spanning the sweep.** `ABANDONED` is terminal (`retryableSessions` selects only PENDING/stale-RUNNING) and 07-17 leaves the window on 07-29, so no repeat. **Generalizable lesson: the first armed sweeps of any as-of replay reach back before its own intent ledger exists — expect one fail-closed terminal row per genuinely-missing pre-ledger session in the window, and do not read those rows as a fault.** The 07-29 sweep should be the true silent no-op. **Still unobserved: a successful replay to `DONE`** (needs an armed session that actually misses); today exercised only the refusal branch. Residual, not fixed and not worth fixing: the refusal alerts an operator about a session nothing can act on — one-time noise by construction.

**RESOLVED — investigation, NOT a bug:** the batch-verify agent's "NFO option 1m capture DEAD (0 rows today)" RED was a **misread** (and the Architect's own subscription-death hypothesis was ALSO wrong). Root cause (Opus-investigated, Architect-DB-confirmed): **there has NEVER been live per-contract option-premium streaming — ZERO option `TICK_AGG` rows on ANY day.** Option premium 1m is populated RETROACTIVELY post-expiry from Upstox (`ExpiredBackfillService`), landing 1–2 days AFTER each weekly expiry; the "334→0 decline" was a `fetched_at` illusion (only futures + indices are WS-pinned; no option pinner exists). "0 today" is EXPECTED — the current 21JUL week hasn't expired. Nothing to fix. If LIVE intraday option premium is ever wanted, it's a NEW `OptionAtmPinner` feature (~160 tokens « 3000 cap), not a repair — **ledger item F-OPT below**, not a chip.

**F-SYNC (new ledger item, ops):** `INSTRUMENT_SYNC` fails ~08:36 IST daily (`local broker DUMP budget saturated`, 07-13/17/20+) — the `kite-dump` limiter is 1 permit/30min and the first attempt burns it then fails on the open circuit; attempts 2–3 have nothing left. **Confirmed INDEPENDENT of F-OPT** (registry stays Sunday-fresh from the weekend full sync; options aren't sync-gated). Non-blocking but recurring. Fix: widen the budget, or retry after the 30-min refresh, or fix the circuit-open-burns-the-permit interaction. → **row G4, DONE — MERGED [#1023](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1023) 2026-07-26** (root cause was the limiter/breaker decoration ORDER, not the budget; the budget is deliberately unchanged).

Armed in `.env` (in place, ACL intact): ntfy + WS at the NOTICE floor, Telegram off; container recreated, env verified in-container, jar fingerprint carries `findLatest`, engine 38 loaded / 0 dropped. Expected live rate: ~1 push/family/day for a persistent condition + 1 per genuine escalation; CONTEXT_SHIFT paces at ~45 min. First-armed-day probe: ACK a live WARN → confirm silence; observe an escalation → confirm the page. **CALIBRATION HALF still evidence-gated** (needs 4+ weeks of quality reports; re-measure the true delivery rate on live data before any floor change). Superseded history: **PARKED until ~2026-08-09 (owner, 2026-07-26).** Delivery half BUILT at the NOTICE floor ([#1040](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1040), converted to DRAFT) but **NOT ARMED** — cross-vendor review returned **7 Majors**. ⚠️ **The headline invalidates a number that was reported to the owner twice:** the "3.57 pushes/day" dry-run counted ROWS, not DELIVERIES. `DataTrustGenerator` uses a per-family/day dedupe key with NO cooldown, `InsightSweeper` runs every 15 min, `InsightRepository`'s upsert returns the existing row id without distinguishing insert from refresh, and `InsightEngine` republishes on every refresh — so ONE persistent CRITICAL condition delivers **~96 pushes/day while showing as a single in-app row**. The build's independent dry-run made the SAME mistake, which is why the two agreed and looked corroborated; agreement between two identical errors is not evidence. **Three Majors compound and gate arming:** (2) republish-on-refresh, (4) ACK/DISMISS bypasses cooldown because the check is OPEN-only, and (5) the noisiest alert CANNOT be muted — the controller rejects all actions when data trust is BLOCKED before reaching MUTE, and DATA_TRUST CRITICALs are themselves BLOCKED. Also open: partial-success double-push, legacy pre-I4 mutes silently becoming GLOBAL, one-way mute in the UI with no expiry. **What holds:** INFO fails closed on null/malformed/unknown severity, shipped defaults deliver nothing, all four env names byte-identical across yml/compose/.env.example, suppressed/cooling/muted rows still persist in-app, Modulith clean (a real `insights`→`notifier` cycle was found and the event record moved into `signals`), delivery async off the insight scheduler. **The calibration half was ALWAYS blocked** — the design gates it on "4+ weeks of quality reports" and there are 14 days. On revisit: fix 2/4/5 first, then RE-MEASURE the true delivery rate before any floor decision. | **DELIVERY DONE+ARMED [#1040](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1040) @ 345c202a · calibration evidence-gated (~4 wks)** |

Count with the test's own regex; red-proof the floor. (b) `ConfigDiff.Op` published a **contract lie** — `before` is null on an add, `after` on a remove, but the generated schema declared both always-present. **Typing an opaque Map does not merely reveal a shape, it COMMITS to it**, so every nullable operand must be checked at conversion time. market-data 26→**16** ([#1131](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1131) @ `870bacc1` — every SINGLE-handler controller: `WorldIndices`, `UpstoxEntitlement`, `FuturesPreOpen`, `ContinuousFuturesAdmin`, `OiBuzz`, `Announcement`, `IvAnalytics`, `Subscriptions`, `Screener`, `OptionsChain.history`). All ten UNCONDITIONAL, and that is provable rather than assumed: nine used `Map.of`, which **throws NPE on a null value**, so every key was already non-null on any response that returned at all. **TWO needed no new record** — `UpstoxAnalyticsClient.Entitlement` and `FuturesPreOpen` already carried the same components, by name and type, in the same sequence the `Map.of` listed them. `Announcement` is the trap and is deliberately NOT `AnnouncementService.Feed`: the wire form differs on two components (`from`/`to` are the `LocalDate.toString()` STRING, and a null `symbol` is emitted as `\"\"`), so returning `Feed` would have silently changed two values. One behaviour delta, in an already-broken path only: a null component `Map.of` would have rejected with an NPE (a 500) now serialises as null. ⚠️ **THE LESSON FROM THIS SLICE — typing an ENVELOPE pulls its ITEM schema into the spec for the first time.** Those item records had never been enumerated (the Map made them `array of object`), so none declared nullability, and FIVE would have published genuinely nullable fields as NON-NULLABLE — a lie in the generated TypeScript. Caught by cross-vendor review, then caught AGAIN when my first fix was still under-scoped. Final non-null sets, each traced to its construction site: `WorldIndex`→`key` ALONE (12 nullable — `isGlobalIndex` admits a row on `instrumentKey != null && segment == GLOBAL_INDEX` and forwards every other master field unchanged, and `UpstoxGlobalInstrumentWireContractTest:53` pins a row carrying only segment+name+instrument_key as an ACCEPTED wire shape); `PreOpenRow`→`symbol`; `ScreenerService.Row`→`exchange,tradingsymbol,latestClose,pastClose,value`; `Announcement`→`date,time,symbol`; `SnapshotRow`→ exactly the V006 DDL's six NOT NULL columns. Spelled `types={\"X\",\"null\"}` (`nullable=true` is a SILENT NO-OP at 3.1) and VERIFIED in the recaptured spec. ⚠️ Third correction from the same review: **`required` is NOT the defect and must stay** — a record component is always PRESENT; what was wrong was the TYPE. The fix is the 3.1 union, never dropping `required`. market-data 16→12 ([#1134](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1134) @ `e580d207` — PreOpen + MarketSurface; `holidays()` items were themselves anonymous Maps, so a holiday row published as a bare object; both newly-exposed item records audited BEFORE typing their envelopes). market-data 12→**6** ([#1138](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1138) @ `5cc45bad` — all six FuturesAnalytics handlers, built by Codex, audited past the receipt). ⚠️ #1138 carries a PLATFORM fix, not slice-local: **`NullableRefCustomizer.appendNullEnumMember`** in common-web-core — under JSON Schema 2020-12 `type` and `enum` BOTH apply, so `type:[\"string\",\"null\"]` beside a 4-member enum REJECTS null; swagger-core never emits the null member for a Java enum, so the customizer appends it at assembly (response-only, 4 tests, red-proofed; blast radius MEASURED across all four specs = 3 lines, all market-data — the platform's only nullable enums). Also: `BankAnalysisCell` nullable at the RECORD level is the correct seam for nullable ARRAY ELEMENTS — `@ArraySchema(types=[object,null])` DESTROYS the $ref and was tried+rejected. backtest 7→**2** ([#1139](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1139) @ `e2fc9188` — Results trades+summary, Jobs list+detail, Indicators series; built by Codex, which CORRECTED the brief: `OiAttributionController.attribution` is ALSO conditional — populated = the empty 10 keys PLUS `runId`+`oiDerived`, a strict superset, so a record would force those present-as-null on the empty path). **Remaining: 12 total = 8 CONVERTIBLE (OptionsAnalytics 4: `oiAnalysis`/`strikeSeries`/`multipleOi`/`optionsChart` · strategy-signal 4: `RiskController` 2, `UniverseController` 1, `NotifierController` 1 — unblocked now #1137 merged) + 4 ASSESSED STOPS (`oiExpiry` 3-vs-4 keys, `openHighStrategy` 3-vs-5, `HeroZeroPremium` 5-vs-16, `OiAttribution` 10-vs-12 — all the HeroZeroPremium class; a record would ADD null keys to the empty response, a wire change needing a shape decision). The TERMINAL floor is 4, not 0 — when the 8 land, D3's Map-return slice is DONE and the 4 stops are the documented end state.** ✅ **MAP-RETURN SLICE CLOSED 2026-08-01 at exactly the predicted terminal floor of 4.** The last 8 landed in three PRs: backtest's two `ResponseEntity`-wrapped returns ([#1188](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1188) @ `3abed95f`), strategy-signal's last four ([#1191](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1191) @ `081d7769`), market-data's last five ([#1190](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1190) @ `4bad0c38`). Final `FROZEN` map, recounted with the test's OWN regex rather than a shell grep: **edge-gateway 0 · market-data 2 · strategy-signal 0 · backtest 2** — and those 4 are exactly the 4 assessed stops (market-data = `oiExpiry` + `openHighStrategy`; backtest = `HeroZeroPremium` + `OiAttribution`), so the end state matches the prediction with nothing unexplained. Deployed + live-verified 2026-08-01: jars fingerprinted for the new classes, 13/13 containers healthy, engine reloaded 38 published / 0 unresolved / 0 load errors, and each converted handler smoke-tested through the gateway (`OiAnalysis` → `{"items":[]}` with `application/json`, `RiskViews` + `JobViews` typed JSON, option-analytics validation failures returning structured `VALIDATION_FAILED` envelopes rather than SPA HTML). ⚠️ **THE RATCHET ITSELF LEAKED THREE TIMES IN ONE DAY, each time a different blind spot in the same regex** — `ResponseEntity<Map<...>>` (closed #1188), a `java.util.`-qualified return (closed #1191), and the fact that a handler-return regex **cannot see an opaque field inside a typed record at all** (`#SystemStatus.jobs`, `#RiskSettingRow.value` — the latter created by #1191's own conversion). A source-text regex is the wrong instrument for this invariant; the spec-based successor is [#1196](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1196), which measured that at regex-floor-zero edge-gateway still published two unconstrained open objects. Keep the regex as a cheap backstop, but never read its floor as "no opaque responses remain". Full record: `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-m40-fresh-entry-risk-cap-gap.md`. **#1214 delivered add-path OBSERVABILITY only** (a `recordPyramidRiskCapBreach` port + per-IST-day-deduped audit, mirroring `recordZeroSizedEntry`) and was relabelled to say so — it does NOT close M40. ⚠️ A pre-existing comment at `SwingBatchEngine.java:495-496` claiming "the ordinary book governor already bounds it" was **FALSE** and is corrected in place; that wrong comment is plausibly why the gap went unnoticed. **Enforcing the cap is HOLD tier** — it would refuse entries that currently fire, changing live P&L behaviour — so it stays an OWNER decision. Owner directed 2026-08-02: **MEASURE FIRST** — backtest how often a 6% cap would actually have bound on real Manas history (which rail binds first: `MAX_OPEN=7` may already bind before 6% does, in which case the gap is largely theoretical AND THAT IS THE FINDING), with the realised P&L of every entry it would have refused. **MEASURED 2026-08-02, TWICE CORRECTED after two cross-vendor review rounds** (round 1: 2 Critical + 1 Major — the first pass measured a 6-slot surrogate, not the real risk ratio, on a non-LIVE-equivalent admission model; round 2: 1 further Critical — the round-1 fix's per-symbol pointer never advanced past warm-up bars, silently excluding almost every established symbol from BOTH arms) — **all prior numbers from both rounds are WITHDRAWN, do not cite them.** The corrected measurement (real chronological replay: lot-rounded quantity sized against MTM equity at entry, the real Chandelier stop trailing every session, real aggregate rupee-risk ÷ CURRENT MTM equity recomputed every session, ONE symbol-shared book across both setups with the live entry-pass-before-exit-pass order, validated to reproduce production's own `simulate()` output exactly — 2,491/2,491 symbols, zero mismatches, both setups — PLUS a new independent portfolio-coverage assertion (2,491/2,491 symbols participate in both arms) added specifically to catch a recurrence of the round-2 defect class — across 2 independent byte-identical runs) now shows: the gap is real (not theoretical) but its net portfolio effect flips sign under correction — **a REAL 6% aggregate cap would have refused 139 entries across 112 distinct sessions** (~4.1% of ~2,750 NSE sessions) that fired under today's live `MAX_OPEN=7` rail (1,065 admitted) — `MAX_OPEN=7` dominates refusal volume overwhelmingly (15,302–13,423 vs. 1,949 aggregate-cap refusals, of which 139 map to a baseline-admitted entry). Refused trades averaged only **+1.13%** (median −0.68%, win rate 46.0% — near-breakeven, NOT clear winners, unlike the withdrawn rounds' claims). Unconfounded whole-portfolio effect of enforcing (both arms use the SAME real risk-based sizing, so no sizing artifact): **CAGR 25.36%→29.44% (+4.08pp), maxDD 42.89%→35.90% (−6.99pp, shallower), Sharpe 1.08→1.21 (+0.13) — a BENEFIT on this measure, reversing the withdrawn rounds' "cost" finding.** No verdict manufactured: the backtest sizes the average-return effect of the cap but cannot evaluate its purpose (bounding CORRELATED risk across simultaneously-open positions in a market-wide shock) — that needs a targeted follow-up, not attempted here — and given the number has now moved twice under review, its SIGN and MAGNITUDE should be treated as provisional pending further independent verification, not as settled. The measurement harness is COMMITTED (not deleted, per the review's instruction) and now opt-in via a system property rather than a source edit: `services/market-data-service/src/test/java/in/arthayantra/marketdata/screener/manas/ManasRiskCapReplayTest.java` (`-Dmanas.replay.enabled=true`). Paper-only today at ₹1.5L/book, so still no urgency to force the enforce/don't-enforce call, but it is no longer accurate to call this gap theoretical. Full evidence + 8 open doubts (topped by an explicit warning about this document's own two-round correction history): `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-m40-risk-cap-backtest.md`. ⚠️ **RE-MEASURED AGAINST SHIPPED SEMANTICS 2026-08-02 — the +4.08pp figure above is WITHDRAWN for the #1221 merge decision; it described a ceiling case production almost never reaches.** Owner-directed before allowing [#1221](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1221) (`feat/manas-fresh-entry-risk-cap` @ `a5b1b63b`) to merge. The v3 measurement charges every held position its CURRENT trailing stop; #1221 charges `ManasGoverningStopCache` when populated and the persisted, never-ratcheted `paper_positions.stop_loss` otherwise — and the cache is empty or near-empty in normal operation for **two independent reasons**: it is only ever WRITTEN for a position whose Chandelier trail has ARMED at +9% (`SwingBatchEngine:882-887` skips on a null `governingStop`; `ExitEvaluator.trailStop` is empty until `arm_pct: 9`) — **ZERO of the six currently-open live Manas positions qualify** (peak gain since entry 0.50–8.19%) — and it is an in-memory `ConcurrentHashMap` destroyed by every restart, while the batch that fills it runs ONCE per weekday (20:05 IST): 21 of the 28 days since Manas go-live carried a merge touching strategy-signal-service, and the container was recreated 2026-08-02 03:58 IST, so the next batch runs COLD. Three of the brief's premises turned out NOT to exist and are recorded as such (the trail VALUE ratchets identically in both — `rollingAtrTrailLevel` accumulates `level.max(c)`; the one-session entry-pass-before-exit-pass LAG is already modelled; an unarmed position charges `initialStop` in both). **Harness re-validated before use** — fidelity 2,491/2,491 both setups, portfolio coverage asserted in all 10 arms AND asserted set-equal between arms (not just count-equal), and the v3 arms reproduce **byte-identically** (1,065 admitted / CAGR 25.36 / maxDD 42.89 / Sharpe 1.08; candidate 1,021 / 29.44 / 35.90 / 1.21; the 139-row marginal set's mean +1.129 / median −0.678 / 46.04%). **Result under shipped semantics: the cap refuses 304 entries across 274 sessions (2.2× v3's 139/112) and REPLACES `MAX_OPEN=7` as the binding rail** (AGG_CAP refusals 1,949→15,472, MAX_OPEN 15,302→123; median aggregate risk at entry 3.82%→5.53%, share facing an already-≥5% book 15.40%→67.70%) — because on a never-ratcheted basis each position charges ~1% of entry-equity forever, making the 6% cap arithmetically close to a 6-position rail. **Whole-portfolio: CAGR 25.36%→24.48% (−0.88pp, a COST — the fourth M40 headline and the second sign reversal), maxDD 42.89%→31.27% (−11.62pp, far SHALLOWER), Sharpe 1.08→1.15 (+0.07).** **Sign robustness FAILS on CAGR and HOLDS on drawdown/Sharpe:** the CAGR sign flips between the cold (−0.88pp) and warm (+4.26pp) brackets, is NON-MONOTONIC across the cold-share sweep (24.48–31.58 with no ordering), flips on exempting **ONE** trade of 304 (`BHARATAGRI@2022-09-19`, +97.10% → CAGR 25.76, above baseline; five exemptions move it 3.18pp, 3.6× the effect being measured), and splits 5/12 by calendar year with a ±25pp range — whereas maxDD is shallower than baseline in **8 of 8** distinct cap arms (12 of 12 counting the drop-k arms) and Sharpe higher in the same 8 of 8 / 12 of 12. Costs (≈0.38pp round trip from this repo's own pinned schedule) leave the refused set at mean +1.71% / median −1.18%, and at a pessimistic 100 bps/side fill at mean −0.19% / median −3.08%; portfolio-level costs are second-order and signed in the cap's FAVOUR (188 fewer round trips). **Live today the rail is immediately reachable: 6 open positions, aggregate risk 5.76% of ₹142,125 equity — a 7th 1% entry lands at ≈6.76%, refused, not marginally.** Exits are genuinely untouched (enumerated every `ManasGoverningStopCache` consumer; `PaperBracketEvaluator` is NOT among them — round 3 reverted the `stop_loss` write). **⚠️ The correlated-shock question the cap exists for remains OUT OF REACH and must not be read as settled by the favourable drawdown number** — maxDD over a historical replay is a realised-path statistic, not a tail bound. **Recommendation: do NOT merge #1221 on a CAGR argument (there is no reliable CAGR number); if it merges it should merge as a DRAWDOWN-control change with the CAGR cost disclosed. Cheapest de-risking step first — land an `effectiveStop` cached-vs-fallback counter (observability only) and let ~2 weeks of live batches replace the guessed cold-share with a measured one.** Full evidence, divergence table, 9 open doubts: `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-m40-remeasure-shipped-semantics.md`. | 07-05 audit §12; §8a; m39 doc §4; `2026-08-02-e4-128-batch-scoping.md`; `2026-08-02-h8-m38-identity-rename.sql`; `2026-08-02-m36-m37-backtest-ab.md` | OWNER (H8+M38 **DONE 2026-08-02** — source corrected #1208 @ `a15bfc37`, and the live identity rename EXECUTED + post-verified by the Architect. Ran `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-h8-m38-identity-rename.sql` as ONE guarded transaction: `UPDATE 1`/`UPDATE 1`, COMMIT, both rows sharing one `updated_at`. **Verified three ways:** every SQL literal diffed byte-for-byte against the two YAMLs BEFORE executing; a second run reported `UPDATE 0`/`UPDATE 0` (the `AND name = <old>` guards make it idempotent); and the live rows now equal the YAML exactly on both `name` and `description`. `strategy.strategies.name` carries a UNIQUE constraint, so a collision would have aborted the whole transaction rather than partially applying. **M36/M37 A/B DONE 2026-08-02 — recommend no change to either** (KEEP arm-day-1; REJECT any thrust-adjacency cap; full evidence in `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-m36-m37-backtest-ab.md`)) |

PART A DONE #874; PART B STILL OPEN.** Occurred TWICE: 2026-07-15 (manual restart to recover; filed here, not built) and **recurred 2026-07-16 within one day** — stack cold-booted 08:57:04, engine `reload()` ran 08:57:19–31 while the token was still expired, all 39 dropped as "resolves to an empty universe", `loaded 0 published strategies` at 08:57:31, owner login landed **08:58:22 = 51s too late**; zero signals/rejections all session while capture was PERFECT (375/375 bars) and every canary stayed green. **True root cause (found 07-16, sharper than the 07-15 note above):** market-data's `FuturesTermStructureService.staleFallback:182` serves an **IN-MEMORY** `lastGood` map (`:78`) on a live-quote failure but **throws 503 when that cache is empty** — a cold boot has an empty cache; a WARM container survives the identical expired-token window off yesterday's cache. That asymmetry is why only cold starts break and why it hid for months. **Part A — SHIPPED [#874](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/874) @ d9f30a8f, deployed + drill-proven live 07-16.** ⚠️ **BUILT DIFFERENTLY FROM THIS ROW'S ORIGINAL SPEC — do not rebuild to the old text.** The original spec (an OR-clause on the 20s reconcile, `hadResolutionFailures && inSession`) was **rejected**: `reload()` calls `bankCache.clear()` unconditionally (`:317`), so a 20s retry loop through a sustained partial outage would wipe every working strategy's warm ta4j bank every 20s — ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 (ledger claim audit shard A): that premise was TRUE when written (2026-07-16) and is FALSE as of today.** [#1226](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1226) @ `e5f465c0` made the eviction SELECTIVE: no `bankCache.clear()` call exists in `SignalEngine` any more (`:941` is only a comment referring to it), and the live behaviour is `SignalEngine.java:995` `bankCache.keySet().removeIf(key -> !freshBankKeys.contains(key))`. The rejection of the original OR-clause spec may still be right on other grounds, but **do not re-derive it from this reason** — the constraint it rests on no longer exists **on the single `signal-eval` thread** — a plausible path into the very eval-starvation stall class we have chased 4× (#634 / A13 RC-1). Built instead: a `kite.status` Redis listener (the channel market-data already publishes; `FeedPipeline.restartFeed:107` self-heals the ticker off the same signal — the engine simply never listened) + a **bounded** 3×~35s retry converging on **`lastReloadEmptyUniverseDrops == 0`** (NOT "loaded > 0": the drop is per-strategy and a breaker re-opening mid-reload leaves a **PARTIAL** load — 32/39 observed live — which a "something loaded" predicate reads as success, stranding the rest silently), plus `start()` reading the level-triggered `kite:session:status` KEY (the channel is edge-only + fire-and-forget, so a `CONNECTED` published before the engine subscribes is lost FOREVER and none ever follows). `futures_screener` is excluded from the drop counter (`resolveScreener` returns empty for BOTH failure and a legitimately empty screen). Bounded by construction ⇒ cannot reintroduce #579. Parity byte-identical (golden 9/9, parity 9/9). **Part B (detection) — BUILT DORMANT via [#1035](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1035) (see G2's `StrategyCoverageWatchdog`, which covers the F10 32-of-39 partial-load class with NOT_LIVE_RESOLVABLE classification; the prerequisite failure-vs-empty split shipped as task_f10a04 #877). Open remainder = the SAME owner arming decision G2 tracks — this row no longer carries independent work.** (Status corrected 2026-07-31 by E2E-01 T4: cell previously said "STILL TODO" and cited #868 `SignalStarvationCanary`, which was RETIRED in #1022.) **NOT a fix:** compose `depends_on: market-data healthy` — container-healthy ≠ term-structure-serving. | 2026-07-16 findings `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-16-session-findings.md` §6.1/§6.3/§7 (drill log in §7.1); `SignalEngine.java` (listener + retry + drop counter), `FuturesTermStructureService.java:78/182`, `SignalStarvationCanary.java:77/87` | clean | **Part A DONE [#874](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/874) · Part B DONE — G2's `StrategyCoverageWatchdog` ARMED live 2026-08-01** |

| G4 | `F-SYNC-instrument-sync-daily-fail` | `INSTRUMENT_SYNC` fails ~08:36 IST daily (`local broker DUMP budget saturated`, 07-13/17/20+): the `kite-dump` limiter is 1 permit/30min, the first attempt burns it then fails on the open circuit, attempts 2–3 have nothing left. **Non-blocking** (registry stays Sunday-fresh from the weekend full sync; options are not sync-gated) but recurring daily noise. Fix = widen the budget, retry after the 30-min refresh, or fix the circuit-open-burns-the-permit interaction | same prose block; promoted 2026-07-25 | clean | **DONE — MERGED [#1023](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1023) 2026-07-26** (3 review rounds, 4 Majors). Root cause was NOT the budget: `KiteCallExecutor.execute` decorated `RateLimiter(CircuitBreaker(call))`, so the permit was acquired BEFORE the breaker was consulted and a breaker-rejected call spent it without touching the network — fatal at 1 permit/30min. Swapped to `CircuitBreaker(RateLimiter(call))` (resilience4j's own documented order). TWO defects that swap introduced or exposed, both caught in review: (a) `RequestNotPermitted` then propagated THROUGH the breaker, so five local saturations could open the SHARED breaker while Kite was healthy — both breakers now `ignore-exceptions` it; (b) preserving the permit did not make the sync RETRY (`CallNotPermittedException` is non-retryable and the 08:30 job runs once), so `morningSyncCatchUp` was added — a bounded 8-attempt sweep 09:05–10:50 IST, skipping only when a sync ended OK *on today's IST date* (`status()` synthesizes an OK from yesterday's `last_seen_at` after a restart, which would otherwise suppress the catch-up entirely). The 1-permit/30min budget is deliberately UNCHANGED. ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 — this row's own "registry stays Sunday-fresh from the weekend full sync" premise is FACTUALLY WRONG: no weekly or weekend sync job exists.** `morningSync` (`0 30 8 * * MON-FRI`) and `morningSyncCatchUp` (`0 5,20,35,50 9,10 * * MON-FRI`) are BOTH `MON-FRI`-only (`InstrumentSyncScheduler:31,61`), and `SyncBootstrap` returns early on the `live` profile. `morningSync`'s cron predates #1023 entirely (original commit `051bb780`), so the weekend silence is unrelated to this fix and has existed since the scheduler was written. The two off-schedule SUCCESS rows the premise was inferred from (`2026-07-26 02:37`, `2026-07-27 00:54` IST) each land minutes after a commit and correspond to no cron — incidental manual/deploy-verification triggers, confirmed by grepping every `runSync`/`triggerAsync`/`@Scheduled` call site. **Verdict UNCHANGED (non-blocking):** weekends have no trading and Monday's normal cron + catch-up refresh the registry before the open. **#1023 is confirmed working in production** (`marketdata.ingest_runs`, 12-day window): zero `budget saturated` rows since 07-20, and 07-29 shows `08:29 FAILURE → 09:04 SUCCESS`, i.e. the catch-up recovering a *different*, correctly-non-retried cause (`no live Kite session`). **Residual sub-defect found while verifying, chipped as `task_4fd8eadc` (not shipped):** `KiteCallExecutor.execute` still wraps `Family.DUMP` in its shared internal `Retry` (max 4), so if the one attempt that reaches Kite fails retryably, attempt 2 needs a second permit from a bucket that will not refill for 30 min — it dies on `RequestNotPermitted` and **masks the real cause behind the same "budget saturated" text**. Red-proofed and fixed locally (skip `Retry.decorateSupplier` for `Family.DUMP`), then discarded: zero occurrences in the observed window means it is latent, not active. |
| G5 | `T12-dedicated-quote-limiter` | Futures-OI capture cadence is ~2-min effective (161 single-minute skips): the batched `quotes()` pass queues behind the shared 1/s kite-quote limiter so the serialized cron skips alternate fires. **Owner recommendation = ACCEPT** (consumers tolerate; 3m buckets covered; severity LOW). A dedicated limiter is an owner-visible rate-budget change. **UNPARKED + FIXED 2026-07-26 (owner overrode the ACCEPT recommendation and picked it) — but NOT as a dedicated limiter, because that would have been wrong.** Recon settled three things at source before any code: (1) **Kite documents the quote endpoint at 1 req/second** (kite.trade/docs/connect/v3/exceptions) and `kite-quote` is already configured to exactly that, so a SECOND dedicated 1/s limiter would put us at 2/s — over Kite's published budget. The row's literal framing was unbuildable. (2) The contention source is not ad-hoc page loads: `MonitorSchedulingConfig.barFlushTaskScheduler`'s own javadoc records that one `OptionsSnapshotService` pass is "~70 batched calls ≈ 70 s at the 1/s limit", and it runs every 2 minutes. **CORRECTED at cross-vendor review:** the primary cause is SCHEDULER STARVATION, not the limiter — both passes sat on the single default `taskScheduler` (pool size 1, ~32 methods), so while the options pass held the thread this cron could not even be INVOKED and its fires were dropped before any permit was requested. Limiter fairness only becomes reachable AFTER the isolation. The demand arithmetic was also incomplete: ~35/min (options snapshot) + up to ~24/min (`scheduledBroadcast`, six underlyings × two quote calls) + 1/min here ≈ **60 of 60 permits/min**, not 36 — completion-based `fixedDelay` keeps actual throughput under the ceiling, so capacity is not proven exceeded, but it is NOT comfortably inside either. resilience4j's `AtomicRateLimiter` is not FIFO, so a waiter with the configured 5 s timeout can be starved straight through the options pass's 70-second permit run and abandon the minute. That is the ~2-min effective cadence, exactly. (3) The obvious fix — wait longer — was unsafe on the default pool: `taskScheduler` is **pool size 1 shared by ~32 scheduled methods**, so parking it would trade a capture gap for a service-wide scheduler stall (the S1–S3 failure mode, #1016). **Built instead:** a dedicated single-thread `oiCaptureTaskScheduler` (same fencing rationale as `barFlushTaskScheduler`; NOT `monitorTaskScheduler`, which is reserved for pure detectors) + a bounded 4-attempt / 3 s-backoff patience ladder around the one batched `quotes()` call, retrying ONLY local limiter saturation (a Kite 429, an open breaker or an auth failure still propagates on the first attempt — retrying those spends budget on a call that cannot succeed). **Rate budget is unchanged at 1/s.** New counters `ay_futures_oi_snapshot_quote_retries_total` + `ay_futures_oi_snapshot_skips_total`: retries advancing with skips flat = the fix working; skips advancing = contention outgrew the patience. ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-02 (ledger claim audit shard A):** this line previously wrote the second meter as `..._skips_total`, whose elision names `ay_futures_oi_snapshot_quote_skips_total` — **a meter that does not exist.** The real name has no `quote_` segment (`FuturesOiSnapshotService.java:70-71`, confirmed live on `/actuator/prometheus`). An operator following the old text greps for nothing and reads the absent meter as FLAT, i.e. as the fix working — failure in the safe-looking direction. `MonitorSchedulingConfig.java:122` already spelled it correctly; only this row was wrong. | `2026-07-25-weekly-bug-queue.md` B9 + item 5 | clean (no budget change) | **DONE — MERGED [#1031](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1031) 2026-07-26, deployed live.** 8/8 `FuturesOiSnapshotServiceTest` green (3 new: retry-then-succeed, bounded-skip-counted, non-saturation-not-retried). ✅ **LIVE-VERIFIED on the first full forward session, 2026-07-27: cadence 372 of 375 minutes (99.2%)**, against 211 / 198 / 187 / 192 / 208 over the five preceding sessions — the ~2-min effective cadence is gone. OI quadrants live a 5th consecutive session (`2026-07-27-session-findings.md` §4) |

The concurrency cliff sits EXACTLY at ₹15,000. **No republish and no migration: the scalper YAMLs came out byte-identical to main.** **[#1075](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1075) is now ONLY the ₹20,000 raise (HOLD, OWNER) — cross-vendor APPROVED at `69a949af` after SIX rounds:** the `budget_inr` ₹15,000→₹20,000 raise + the deployment cap and per-sub-account ceiling, all three landed at ONE seam (`PaperService.openOrder`, the sole writer — emission cannot gate them because the rail needs the candidate's SIZE, which does not exist yet). **The review found 4 further defects after the first two, THREE of them created by my own fixes:** refusing straddle leg 2 left leg 1 open = a NAKED directional position (fixed by an atomic `openPair`); both caps were unlocked read-check-write races (per-book advisory lock); sub-account SELECTION still ran outside that lock so 5 accounts behaved as 1 (assignment moved under the lock); and the new wrapper took `book → anchor` while the manual path takes `anchor → book` — a **DEADLOCK on the money path** (fixed by `lockAnchorsBeforeBook`; round 6 confirmed by global scan that no `book → anchor` path remains). I caught 2 more myself: the candidate was valued as raw `price×qty` while `capitalUsed` uses `usageFor` (~8× overstatement on a short option), and a test that passed for the wrong reason. **LESSON, and it repeated all night: adding enforcement converts previously-harmless gaps into live defects** — routing blindness was harmless until a ceiling existed; independent legs were harmless until something could refuse one. Same shape as #1067's "a dead path hides every defect downstream of it". **OWNER DECISION: ₹20,000 against a ₹30,000 sub-account allocation means each account holds ONE position, so scalper concurrency becomes 5** (book cap would allow 6, `max_open` says 20); at ₹15,000 the binding limit was 8. The fix therefore carries the OBSERVATION in a durable Redis HASH, not a position. ⚠️ **FIVE review rounds found SIX ways a live pager could go SILENT, every one introduced by an individually-correct fix and none reachable by a green suite:** asymmetric 25% cap (capped the held half, not the incoming partner, so a genuinely frozen bucket corroborated as benign); restart losing a held mismatch; a one-bucket look-back that still skipped it when two buckets completed; a TTL key under `volatile-lru` (evictable, plus read-failure indistinguishable from absence); **delete-before-report** (a crash after Redis accepted the delete but before the log erased the alarm — now report-first, so a crash duplicates rather than erases); and **recovery indexed by live series**, so a rolled-away contract's alarm was unreachable — the case persistence exists for (restart) combined with the thing that changes across one (which contracts are live). A proactive audit then found a THIRD lose-information site: `classified.put()` marked a bucket handled BEFORE acting on it. **`volume-tolerance-pct` STAYS 0.0** even now: the pct arm is per-bucket, so at 5% it absorbs the thick half while firing the thin one, manufacturing a false UNPAIRED WARN (23,000 vs 7,062, pinned by test). Eleven defect shapes enumerated and red-proofed; operators should expect MORE WARNs after a restart, not fewer (fail-closed on a cold lot cache). **Method lesson: TWO of this PR's three red-proofs were WRONG when first written** — fixtures that satisfied their assertion through an unrelated path, invisible to a green run, caught only because a deliberate mutation failed to red. History: RE-NARROWED TWICE; the residue is provably benign and the ROW HAD BECOME A TOLERANCE ROW, not a correctness one. 07-28: WARNs tripled to 10, the 09:15 signature did NOT recur, and the 2 unpaired events moved to the session's two thickest buckets (+2,730 / −2,600 = 42 and 40 lots). **2026-07-29 is the cleanest session in the series: 6 WARNs, ALL of them exact ± pairs on consecutive buckets, ALL shortfalls exact ×65 multiples, ZERO unpaired events** — the documented benign boundary straddle (README §3.17), with no unexplained residue left at either end. **The 09:15 signature #981 targeted has now failed to recur on two consecutive warm-process sessions.** What survives is a tolerance defect: the largest shortfall (**16,835 = 259 lots**) is **3.5%** of a 476,840 opening bar — comparable to 07-28's 2.5%/2.4% and 07-27's 2.7% — so every one of the 6 events was provably benign **and every one still alarmed**, because `artha.signals.partial-bucket-canary.volume-tolerance` is an ABSOLUTE 650 (+ a ≤10% relative arm). **Proposal, unchanged in substance since 07-28 and now strongly evidenced: make the tolerance scale with bar size.** ⚠ Do NOT simply raise the absolute — a thin frozen bar must still fire, which is why the relative arm exists; the change is to the ABSOLUTE arm's basis, not its removal. The post-#981 rollover-baseline code read is still outstanding but is now LOW value: two clean negative controls make a second contributor at the open unlikely. `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-29-session-findings.md` §6.1 / T23. **DELIVERED (PR [#1180](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1180)):** the fix is NOT a bigger number — the per-event gate was reading each bucket in isolation while §3.17's benign signature is a PAIR property, so a non-benign bucket is now HELD one bucket and suppressed TOGETHER with its equal-and-opposite partner (both halves or neither — a half-suppressed pair presents as the unpaired shape the runbook teaches operators to read as a real defect). Corroboration needs all five of: immediately-following bucket, opposite signs, **BOTH halves ≤25% of their OWN bucket** (so a frozen bar is never explained away, whichever side it lands on — the cross-vendor review found the cap was applied only to the held half, which let a genuine frozen partner be cancelled by an earlier small skew), both magnitudes exact multiples of the contract lot (instrument master, never a hardcoded 65; a lot not yet resolved keeps the rule CLOSED), and an uncancelled residue inside BOTH `volume-tolerance` and 10% of the larger half — so the suppression can hide at most the absolute tolerance the canary already treats as noise, adding no new blind spot. **A hold is taken only if it was stored durably first** (Redis, no migration; `save` refusing ⇒ WARN now, so Redis-down degrades to exactly pre-G9 behaviour), and the carried observation is restored on the next cold sweep. ⚠️ **Stored with NO TTL, deliberately** — the instance runs `maxmemory-policy volatile-lru` (`deploy/docker-compose.yml:72-83`) under which only keys carrying an expiry are eviction candidates, so a TTL'd unreported observation could be dropped by memory pressure *or* the clock; the key is deleted on RESOLUTION instead. A read FAILURE is tri-stated as UNKNOWN (never "absent"), so the restore is retried and the canary refuses to defer until it knows — one Redis blip can no longer swallow a mismatch. **Write-ahead ordering throughout**: the counter/log fires BEFORE the durable delete, so a crash between them can duplicate a report but never erase one (three sites had the losing order — both resolution paths and, found by auditing for a third instance, the `classified` marker, which retired a bucket before it had been acted on). Recovery enumerates the durable index (one Redis HASH, `HGETALL`) **independently of the live series set** and reports any hold whose contract is gone — indexing by live series meant a monthly roll stranded an unreported mismatch for ever, since "is this alarm reachable" and "is this contract still trading" are unrelated questions. The canary also has its **own scheduler** (`partialBucketTaskScheduler`): it left the `monitorTaskScheduler` detector fence when it acquired external dependencies, and every store call is time-bounded (2 s) on a bounded-queue executor with cancel-on-timeout, so a stalled Redis cannot starve sibling detectors, pile up abandoned writes, or resurrect a resolved observation. ⚠️ **Re-deriving a lost hold is impossible, so no look-back of any depth would have worked** — the 3m series is `rangeRolledFromOneMinute` over the very same `candles` 1m rows the 1m series is re-warmed from (`CandleQueryService:103-105`), so after a restart a re-read compares DB against DB and is benign by construction; the diverging side (live tick-agg, never revised) died with the process. Also: the lot lookup is a NON-BLOCKING cache with an off-pool prefetch — the sweep runs on the single-threaded monitor scheduler whose contract forbids blocking external calls. **Honest latency:** a >25% skew still WARNs by T+60 s unchanged; a same-sign drift by T+4 m; a never-arriving partner by T+5 m. All six 07-29 events replay to 0 WARNs / 3 straddles (red-proofed at 6.0 against the pre-change logic); unpaired, same-sign-drift, mismatched-magnitude, non-lot-multiple, never-arriving-partner, **capped-partner frozen bucket**, **held half across a multi-bucket restart**, **undurable hold**, **unreadable store**, **crash between report and durable delete** and **hold on a rolled-away contract** shapes each still WARN, every one mutation- or red-proofed. ⚠️ A cold process's lot cache is EMPTY, so the first pair after a restart WARNs both halves rather than suppressing — two safe behaviours composing into extra noise, never silence; pinned so the enumeration cannot drift into claiming a suppression guarantee that only holds with a pre-warmed fixture. New meter `ay_signal_partial_bucket_straddle_total`. **Complementary to, not a substitute for, [#1168](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1168)** — that PR rebases the absolute arm on bar size, this one changes what counts as ONE event; `volume-tolerance-pct` is untouched here |

On 07-29 the **minimum** of the first ten 3m bars was **32,760 — already 2.2× the session median of 15,015**. G12's #1111 probe cannot see this either — it tests DISTINCTNESS, and this operand moves. ⚠️ **This is the SECOND one-constituent near-miss** (07-28 also max exactly 32; 07-21 max 31), and over 8 sessions the dot is **0% on FIVE, ~100% on TWO, 0.2% on ONE — never in between**, because `advances` is a market-wide scalar shared by every row of the session. **So its 1.0 of an 18.80 denominator is a ±5.3 pp per-session BIAS, not a per-bar discriminator** — the same shape as `iv_abs_band`'s per-day step function (G12), reached by a different mechanism. ⚠️ Do NOT treat this as a threshold-tuning row on its own: `>32` on a ~50-name universe is a doctrine number, and moving it is an entry-gate loosening — **which the T1/T7/G13/G10 prior says has lost money four times out of four.** The question this row asks is whether a market-wide scalar belongs in a PER-BAR confluence composite at all, which is a design question, not a knob. Method promoted to README **§3.28** (a dot at 0% is now three explanations deep) + §4.1 | `docs/signal-analysis/2026-07-30-session-findings.md` §3.2 / T30 | **HOLD (any change to the operand or its weight changes which signals fire)** | ✅ **DONE — near-miss probe MERGED [#1169](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1169) @ 946e0c11, deployed 2026-08-01.** The FOURTH dot-input state (alive + moving + never crossing) is now visible on `GET /api/v1/signal-rejections/dot-health` with a `near-miss` badge, disjoint from the G12 frozen state by construction. **SCOPE HELD: no operand, threshold or weight moved** — `>32` is a doctrine number and moving it is an entry-gate loosening (lost money 4 of 4 measured times); `sweep()` is byte-identical so the state never pages. ⚠️ **Review found the detector was NOT session-wide while CLAIMING to be:** it read only the newest 200 rejection rows, and under 38-strategy fan-out that covers a handful of bars, so a genuine 09:25 crossing could age out and the dot be flagged never-crossing later the same session — false positives AND false negatives from a probe whose only value is trustworthiness. Fixed with a session-wide `DISTINCT ON (bar_time, side)` read bounded to the IST day, collapsing fan-out IN THE DB, fired at most once and only when a probe reaches the near-miss branch (a dead/frozen dot pays nothing). Cost justified rather than assumed: `signal_rejections` is a PLAIN OLTP table with the right index, so the Timescale 2.18.2 sorted-merge trap structurally cannot apply. **Second review finding REVERSED the Architect's own call:** strictness (0 crossings) was chosen to avoid false positives, but that reasoning applies to PAGERS — this badge never pages and touches no money, so a false negative preserves exactly the blindness G16 was filed to kill. Tolerance is now 2% of distinct (bar, side) verdicts with a floor of one, derived from the gap between the one-sided sessions (0%, 0.2%) and the nearest genuinely-rare firing dot (`oi_spurt` 4.9%), aggregated per (bar, side) so fan-out cannot skew it. **The row's actual question — whether a market-wide scalar belongs in a per-bar composite at all — remains an OWNER design call, unchanged.** Prior state: **OPEN — NEW 2026-07-30.** ⚠️ Filed by THIS row's promotion rule, not by the routine that found it: the 07-30 findings doc said "Needs a §0 group-G row" and #1125 shipped without one — a finding that lives only in a dated doc is invisible to the next enumeration. Same PR also landed README §3.27, which #1125 claimed as promoted but never wrote (the second dangling promotion in two sessions). |
| G17 | `T14-sign-aware-margin-invariant` | **A persisted rejection row can contradict itself — it can record a BLOCK whose operand actually CLEARED its threshold — and nothing asserts otherwise.** Origin 2026-07-20 §6.3: **7 rows logged a first-block with a POSITIVE `blocking_margin`**. The obvious fix (assert `blocking_margin < 0` on persist) is WRONG as stated, which is why this needs design and not just a guard: 2026-07-23 §2.3 established that `vwap-distance` first-blocks with a positive margin **and is CORRECT** — the sign that means "blocked" depends on the rail's comparison direction, so the invariant must be **sign-aware per rail**, deriving the expected sign from the gate's own operator rather than assuming every rail is a floor. Defect is INTERMITTENT (0 self-contradicting rows on 07-29/07-30/07-31 — three clean sessions running, `composite-pass ∩ composite-blocked = 0`), which is exactly why an assertion is worth more than another session of eyeballing: a diagnostic that only misfires occasionally is the kind that gets explained away. Scope = diagnostic only; it changes no gate outcome and no money path | `2026-07-20-session-findings.md` §6.3 · refined `2026-07-23-session-findings.md` §2.3 · carried in every findings doc since | clean (diagnostic) | ✅ **DONE — MERGED [#1171](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1171) @ b7246a3e, deployed 2026-08-01.** A self-contradicting rejection row (a BLOCK whose operand actually CLEARED its threshold) is now counted + WARN-logged, never thrown — diagnostic only, no gate outcome and no money path moves. **The expected sign is DERIVED, not declared:** `RailMarginSign` rides `GateOutcome`, stamped by the gate that performed the comparison, and flows to the persist seam — so **the hand-maintained `RailMarginSigns` table is DELETED**, and the drift artifact this row existed to eliminate no longer exists in any form. Nine gates plus the composite declare signs, each probed on both sides of its threshold; the three side-dependent/conjunction rails are proven to block with BOTH signs. Default is `UNSIGNED`, so silence never becomes an accusation. ⚠️ **Review caught two Majors:** (1) the first cut matched rail NAMES only, never binding a registry entry to the actual operator wiring — so once `vwap_distance_min_frac` is armed, that genuinely two-bound rail's legitimate NEGATIVE-margin blocks would all have been counted as self-contradictory, i.e. the diagnostic crying wolf on correct behaviour; now `vwapDistance` computes its sign per evaluation (ceiling when only `maxFrac` is set, `UNSIGNED` once `minFrac` is armed). (2) the WARN sat SYNCHRONOUSLY on the sole evaluation thread ahead of `queue.submit`, violating the bounded async-writer doctrine — lookup and counter stay on the caller (they must survive a queue drop), the WARN moved inside the submit. Net one FEWER file. Prior state: **OPEN — promoted to a ledger row 2026-07-31 (chip task_e2e01h, owner: PROMOTE not WONT).** It had been carried session-to-session as "PROPOSED (carried)" since 07-20 with **no ledger row at all**, which the 2026-07-31 E2E audit flagged as a promotion-rule violation (docs-drift #7): the six-location enumeration recipe cannot see a proposal that lives only in findings tables, so it rode eleven sessions invisibly. Startable — needs the per-rail expected-sign map derived from the gate operators, then the assertion at the `recordRejection` persist seam |

**Republish trap worth keeping:** "latest DRAFT vs published" is the WRONG divergence test — the
seeder's `resyncConfig` dedupes against `latestVersion(strategyId)` *of any status*, so an
already-current strategy keeps a months-old leftover draft that the naive query flags. It flagged
all 38 and would have *reverted* `scalp-gap-theory-nifty` to a 07-06 draft missing the armed
`relative-volume-floor` tag. Correct test: **latest version row ≠ `published_version_id`** (28 hits,
all verified to gain the bands and lose nothing before publishing). Also: `Invoke-WebRequest -Method
Post` with no `-ContentType` sends `x-www-form-urlencoded` and the publish endpoint answers **500**
(not 415) — pass `-ContentType 'application/json' -Body '{}'`; the unmapped
`HttpMediaTypeNotSupportedException` is a small error-handling gap (chip-worthy, not filed).

(task_392b3672); `hasBoundingExit` reachability hardening (task_85543821) — both ran in separate
sessions. New chip from the republish pass: **task_9ffe390d** (a wrong `Content-Type` returns 500,
not 415 — unmapped `HttpMediaTypeNotSupportedException` in the shared `GlobalExceptionHandler`).

A point-in-time funnel has NO data on any real backtest window. **The real work is an OWNER/design item, not a chip:** historical screen backfill (bhavcopy + geometry recompute per date) + an `asOf`-threaded resolver (the endpoint already accepts `asOf`) + a runner that REPLAYS the funnel rather than `get(0)`. (2) **STILL OPEN — the structural half:** 5 of the 6 remaining required contexts come from PATH-FILTERED workflows (`contracts` from ci-contracts.yml, `gitleaks` + `build-test (×3)` from ci-java.yml), so they never report on a PR that touches none of those paths and such a PR is blocked forever — proven on docs-only #1006, where ONLY `e2e` + `verdict` ran. `e2e` (ci-e2e.yml) is the only required context that runs on every PR; `react` is path-filtered too, which is why swapping the name in would rebuild the same trap. Fix needs a design call with a COST tradeoff (owner-facing): drop the path filters (every docs PR then pays the ~8 m market-data shard — CLAUDE.md calls that shard the PR floor), or add always-reporting shims / a change-detection `changes` job + `if:`-gated heavy jobs reporting one stable check name (watch the skipped-job-vs-required-check semantics and the double-report race when `paths` and `paths-ignore` both match a mixed PR). Until then: code-touching PRs should merge normally; docs-only PRs still need `--admin`. **STRUCTURAL HALF DONE 2026-07-26 — owner picked the change-detection option.** The `paths:` filters are GONE from ci-java.yml + ci-contracts.yml; both workflows now always trigger, a cheap `changes` job classifies the diff (merge base on a PR, `github.event.before` on a main push) and the heavy jobs carry `if: needs.changes.outputs.* == 'true'`. **The load-bearing fact, sourced from GitHub's docs, not assumed:** "If a job within a workflow is skipped due to a conditional, it will report its status as `Success`" whereas "if a workflow is skipped due to path filtering [...] checks associated with that workflow will remain in a `Pending` state" and block the PR — the two skip mechanisms are NOT interchangeable, which is exactly why the old filter was unsatisfiable. Job NAMES are unchanged, so all six required contexts keep resolving. **`gitleaks` is deliberately left UNGATED** (a secret can land in any file type; a docs-only PR is precisely when you still want the scan, and it costs ~30 s). Classifier is **fail-safe = RUN**: an unresolvable base (force-push, first push, unanticipated event shape) runs the full suite rather than declaring nothing changed. Cost answer to the row's own tradeoff: a docs-only PR now pays ~30 s of gitleaks instead of the ~8 m market-data shard, and needs no `--admin`. ✅ **THIRD CAUSE RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (owner ruled it ACCIDENTAL).** `lock_branch` set to `false` via a full-object `PUT .../branches/main/protection`. The payload was built by mirroring the live GET field-by-field, and a before/after diff confirmed **`lock_branch` was the ONLY setting that moved** — all six required contexts, `strict: true`, `enforce_admins: false`, `required_linear_history`, `required_conversation_resolution`, `allow_force_pushes/deletions` and `restrictions` all byte-identical (the protection API replaces the WHOLE object, so a sloppy PUT silently weakens other controls — always diff after). Also confirmed there are **no repository rulesets** (`GET /rulesets` returns `[]`), so classic protection was the only mechanism in play and the diagnosis was complete. **Why it read as accidental:** with `enforce_admins: false` the lock stopped nobody — the owner bypassed it trivially and is the only person who can merge — while defeating the entire six-context required-check setup on every merge. Superseded note, kept for the record: ⚠️ **THIRD CAUSE, found while merging #1030 — `main` has `lock_branch: {"enabled": true}`, i.e. the branch is READ-ONLY.** With `enforce_admins: false`, that means EVERY merge must use `--admin`, which bypasses ALL required checks including a genuinely red one — so the "no more `--admin`" outcome this row is really about is STILL NOT DELIVERED, and neither the dead-context fix nor the path-filter fix could ever have delivered it alone. Proof: #1030 had all six required contexts reporting SUCCESS and `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, yet a plain `gh pr merge --squash` returned `the base branch policy prohibits the merge` with `mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED`; it merged only with `--admin`. Owner decision chipped as `task_7bea266e`: was the lock deliberate? If accidental, disabling it is what makes #1030 pay off; if deliberate, record it in CLAUDE.md so no future session re-files this as a bug, and accept that required checks are advisory. **Do not read this row as "docs-only PRs now merge without `--admin`" — they do not.** ✅ **SKIP PATH PROVEN 2026-07-26 on [#1032](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1032)** (docs-only, based on the merged main): `build-test (strategy-gateway)` **2 s**, `(backtest)` **4 s**, `(market-data)` **6 s** — all three materialised and PASSED rather than never reporting (market-data alone was the ~8-minute PR floor before) — `contracts` **5 s**, and `gitleaks` still ran ungated at **9 s** exactly as designed. `e2e` is unchanged because it was never path-filtered. Superseded note, kept for the record: ⚠️ **NOT YET PROVEN ON THE SKIP PATH:** the PR that ships this change edits both workflow files, which are themselves in the classifier's path set, so it runs everything and exercises only the `true` branch. The `false` branch (shards report Success-by-skip, PR mergeable without `--admin`) must be observed on the next genuinely docs-only PR before this row is trustworthy. | **CLOSED 2026-07-26 — all THREE causes fixed; a plain `gh pr merge --squash` now works** |

| task_0bed1621 | 2026-07-26 | **Expose `SignalEngine.lastBarReceivedAtMs` + `lastBarEvaluatedAtMs` as an operator-readable surface** (Micrometer gauges on the existing 8082 actuator, and/or a small typed endpoint). These two are the ONLY unconfounded engine-liveness oracles, and NOTHING outside the process can read them today. Consequence, established across 4 cross-vendor review rounds on the canary retirement: **on a fully quiet session, engine liveness is currently UNPROVABLE by an operator or agent.** Every proxy admits a false PASS — rejections are direction-dependent; `strategy.signals` mixes the swing BATCH engine; `subscriber_health_events` is write-only fail-soft so absence proves nothing; `signal_eval_outcomes` freshness only proves the ROLLUP thread (its own scheduler, never the eval thread) is alive; and a thread dump parked on `LinkedBlockingQueue.take()` cannot distinguish quiet tape from a dropped Redis listener, because candle RECEIPT is what submits the drain. SCOPE: read surface ONLY — no new detector in the same PR (one was just retired for keying on a confounded signal). Parity ladder applies (edits live `SignalEngine`): `GoldenDeterminismTest` lives in `libs/strategy-engine`, NOT backtest-service. | **DONE 2026-07-26** — two Micrometer gauges on strategy-signal's existing 8082 actuator: `ay_signal_bar_received_age_seconds` and `ay_signal_bar_evaluated_age_seconds`, both publishing an AGE IN SECONDS (directly comparable to a threshold; no clock-skew reconciliation between container and reader). Pre-registered at boot for the #895 reason — a lazily-created meter would leave a MISSING series indistinguishable from a dead engine, the same ambiguity one level up. **A NEGATIVE value is not a small age:** `-1` = no bar EVER received/evaluated on this boot (the stamps are boot-seeded for the canary's grace, so a plain age would read ~0 there and look identical to a fresh bar), and anything BELOW `-1` = the clock stepped backwards. Negatives are deliberately NOT clamped — an earlier draft floored them at 0, which hid a genuine clock fault behind a value that reads as freshly alive. **The operator verdict requires BOTH gauges:** received fresh + evaluated GROWING is an EVAL STALL, which a received-only reading (the first draft) would have called PASS. READ SURFACE ONLY, no detector (a separate owner decision). README §4.3 step 4 and both `session-analysis` SKILL.md copies now key the quiet-session verdict on the BOTH age gauges instead of the thread dump (received alone is not enough — bars can arrive while `signal-eval` is wedged), and the 'liveness is UNPROVABLE' language is gone — re-grepped, not assumed, after twice claiming it was gone while blocks still carried it. Parity ladder run and green (Golden 9/9, Parity 9/9) — though note it is structurally NOT exposed: the live `SignalEngine` has no class in `libs/strategy-engine` and is not on the golden replay path (`TickwiseGoldenRunner` is), so the gauges could not have moved a vector. |
