At the observed D≈37s that put attempt 3 at ~144s — both faults (~73s, ~81s) cleared with 60-70s of margin. **Landing on attempt 3 is the EXPECTED outcome for any fault in (72s, 144s].** The REAL argument: only the `D→0` case is GUARANTEED (breaker OPEN ⇒ instant reject), and at 3 attempts that floor was `2×35s = 70s` — **SHORTER than both measured faults**. The drills recovered only because their failing reloads happened to be slow; a fast-failing chain would have missed both. **8 attempts ⇒ guaranteed floor 245s ≈ 3× the ~81s worst case**, still bounded (an unbounded predicate reintroduces #579); delay stays 35s to outlive the kite-rest breaker. Sized against the FLOOR, not the distribution — which holds under either reading of the drill data, so the "measured distribution" this row asked for is not needed. |

Reachable shape: attempt 1 → `drops=20, loaded=19`; attempt 2 (+35s) → Kite regressed → `loaded=0`; attempt 3 → still bad → exhausts and returns, **engine frozen at 0 for the session where it would have been 19 without the retry**. Narrow trigger (needs monotonic degradation across 70s; the normal login trajectory improves as the breaker heals), bounded to one session, recovered by the 08:40 cron. Observed in-vitro during the 07-16 drill (boot 32 → attempt 1 = 0). Minimum useful fix: on the exhaustion path, compare `drops` against the first attempt's and say so in the `log.error` — that is the signal the retry actively harmed. Better: don't install a strictly-worse reload result. | **DONE — [#892](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/892) @ 27bd41fb, MERGED 2026-07-17 (deploy held for post-close, with task_f10a01).** Took the "better" option, then went further after 4 cross-vendor rounds. **ROOT CAUSE the chips only circled: `liveCandidates > 0 && loadedCount == 0` is a GAP INFERENCE, and a gap cannot distinguish "correctly loaded nothing" from "everything failed."** Health now counts failures EXPLICITLY (`unresolvedDrops == 0 && loadErrors == 0`); `loadedCount` is absent from health entirely — stricter adherence to "`unresolved == 0`, NEVER `loaded > 0`" than the first draft managed. That also closed the live hole where a reload whose strategies all took an UNCOUNTED skip reported "0 loaded, 0 drops" and logged **"resolved every universe" over a DEAD engine**. Keep-best is **per-strategy**: own-failure ⇒ last-good retained AT ITS REGISTRY-WALK POSITION; RESOLVED_EMPTY stand-aside ⇒ always installed; absent/disabled ⇒ dropped (structural, never inferred — they `continue` before the retain call). `start()` now takes the boot outcome and arms on `!boot.healthy()` (it previously discarded it and armed only on `drops > 0`, so an all-load-error boot gave 0/0, never armed, and stayed **dead all session**). **Review caught 2 defects the FIX ITSELF introduced:** (1) a legitimately-empty screener was judged unhealthy ⇒ suppressed ⇒ engine kept emitting against YESTERDAY's movers — a money regression; (2) retained entries appended behind successes, but `SignalEngine:903` iterates `loaded` in order and `RiskService:114-118` checks a SHARED per-book max-open slot, so the same registry could trade B after a retry but A after a clean reload. `wouldRegressTheEngine` was ultimately DELETED — per-strategy retention subsumed it. Known asymmetry recorded: `loadErrors` counts transient market-data work AND permanent `StrategyCompiler.compile` alike, so a permanent config error exhausts the chain every CONNECTED (deliberate — a loud wrong-config beats a quiet dead engine). |

The PR body is the right home; it just needs to bind. | **DONE — [#884](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/884) @ 7c8a30ad, MERGED 2026-07-17.** `.github/workflows/ci-review-verdict.yml` — a body-only check (~2s, no checkout) requiring one verbatim `Cross-vendor review: <verdict>` line. `SKIPPED (<reason>)` is a **first-class PASS** (a rule that cannot express the legitimate case gets ignored in the illegitimate one); `hotfix/*` exempt BY DESIGN; NOT a required check, so `--admin` bypasses — the point is to make an omission VISIBLE, not hold the repo hostage to a regex. Regex proven against real bodies pre-merge: #878 PASS, #877/#879 FAIL, `SKIPPED ()` rejected. |
| task_b3b59719 | 2026-07-17 | **`ci-contracts`' breaking-change gate is STRUCTURALLY BLIND on exactly the PRs it exists for.** `.github/workflows/ci-contracts.yml:39-50` — the step named "Diff committed vs regenerated (fail on BREAKING)" mounts `-v "$PWD/contracts:/committed"` (the BRANCH's spec, which the PR just updated) against `-v "$PWD/services/$svc/target/contracts:/fresh"` (the BRANCH's code). **Both sides are the branch. It never compares against `main`.** So it only has teeth on a PR that changes code and does NOT re-capture the spec — and the VERY NEXT STEP (`:52-58`) instructs you to re-capture (`::warning:: ... refresh it with -Dcontracts.capture=true`), which CLAUDE.md + ship-a-change also require in the same PR. **The workflow's own instruction blinds its own gate**, on essentially every PR that changes an endpoint. The header comment at `:1-6` ("BREAKING changes fail") is therefore false. PROOF: the Opus review of the D3 reference-data fetcher ran openapi-diff against MAIN's spec and got `GET /api/v1/instruments/indices/{index}/constituents -> Schema: Broken compatibility`, exit 1 — while ci-contracts passed green. (That change is provably non-breaking ON THE WIRE — an IT pins the JSON keys — so the gate is what's broken, not the diff.) WHY IT MATTERS: several consumers read JSON keys OVER THE WIRE with no compile-time link (e.g. strategy-signal's `registry/UniverseResolver:143-152` reads `asOf`/`items`/`exchange`/`tradingsymbol` from market-data's constituents endpoint); a renamed key silently breaks live universe resolution and no compiler catches it — this gate is supposed to be that net. FIX: diff against the MERGE BASE (`git show $(git merge-base HEAD origin/main):contracts/$svc.openapi.json`) vs the branch's code; keep the existing committed-vs-code step as the separate "did you forget to re-capture?" warning. MUST have a first-class escape for an INTENTIONAL break (label or PR-body token, mirroring `SKIPPED (<reason>)` in ci-review-verdict.yml / task_07199525) — a hard fail with no override gets the workflow disabled the first time it is inconvenient, which is worse than the status quo. Expect the first run to surface pre-existing incompatibilities accrued while it was blind — report, don't mass-fix; the owner decides. Do NOT block `hotfix/*`. **Prove the fixed gate can FAIL** (rename a response key -> RED; add a field -> green). Found by the Opus cross-vendor review 2026-07-17; verified at source by the Architect. Same family as task_587984d1 + task_07199525: a gate that cannot answer the question it claims to. | **DONE — [#888](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/888) @ 253aaf42, MERGED 2026-07-17** (e2e admin-merged past the documented signals/ws pair: the diff is `.github/workflows/ci-contracts.yml` + `CLAUDE.md` ONLY — no app source, so it is formally unreachable from a Playwright run; every other gate green, incl. the new gate self-validating on its own PR). Breaking gate now diffs the **MERGE BASE**'s spec vs the branch's code, so re-capturing no longer blinds it; committed-vs-code survives as a warn-only "did you forget to re-capture?"; escape hatch `Contract break: APPROVED (<reason>)` with a mandatory non-empty reason (mirrors ci-review-verdict's `SKIPPED (<reason>)`), `hotfix/*` exempt; `fetch-depth: 0` + fail-loud if the merge base won't resolve. Proven: endpoint-removal + re-capture = OLD gate green / NEW gate RED; additive stays green. **Cross-vendor review caught a real Major:** every nonzero openapi-diff exit became `broke`, and the hatch waives `broke` — so an exit-2 tool failure PASSED whenever the PR carried a token (a gate reporting success having checked nothing). Fixed: exit 1 is the ONLY verdict, any other nonzero hard-fails BEFORE the hatch; matrix simulated across rc=0/1/2/125 × {token, no token}. **⚠️ RESIDUE (measured, reported, NOT fixed — chip task_ade97df8):** the gate still CANNOT see a renamed response key — openapi-diff 2.1.7 does not diff `openapi: 3.1.0` schemas at all (retype string→integer reads "No differences"; the same bytes relabelled 3.0.1 correctly fail), and springdoc emits no `required` for record responses so removing/renaming an OPTIONAL response key is backward-compatible by construction. The header + CLAUDE.md now state this instead of lying about it. |

| task_fbc8e4bd | audit whether OTHER deterministic guards are surefire-rerun-maskable, after #1115 exempted `ModularityTest` only | 2026-07-30 | **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — PREMISE FALSE, no change shipped.** Candidates were `MapReturnRatchetTest`, `GatewayRouteAllowlistTest` and `ContractCaptureTest`×4. **Measured, not reasoned:** broke MapReturnRatchetTest's floor and ran it with `-Dsurefire.rerunFailingTestsCount=2` and WITHOUT any exemption — it ran 3 times, failed all 3, BUILD FAILURE, **no `Flakes:` marker**. So it was never maskable. ⚠️ **The masking is NOT a property of "structural tests"; it is a property of Spring Modulith's STATIC CACHE of the computed `ApplicationModules`, which makes the retry skip recomputation.** Anything that re-derives its verdict every run (a file scan, a spec re-capture) fails identically on retry and needs no exemption. `ContractCaptureTest` is doubly wrong to add: it recomputes AND its Spring context is a legitimate flake source, so exempting it would convert infrastructure noise into hard build failures. **A POM change was written and then REVERTED** — it would have been config for an impossible case (working principle 2). The durable output is the scope note now in `pom.xml` beside the exemption: *before adding a test here, ask whether it CACHES its verdict across runs in one JVM.* |
| task_06ad72b6 | 2026-07-31 | **Bhavcopy catch-up deadlock — the #1016 fix had the WRONG SCOPE and the deadlock came back.** `BhavcopyBackfillIntegrationTest.aTransientlyMissedDayIsReProbedAndHeals` hit `ERROR: deadlock detected` on ALL THREE surefire attempts on PR #1138's CI shard (a retry loop cannot mask a lock-order problem). `BhavcopyStartupCatchup`'s own javadoc documents this exact deadlock from #1016, fixed then by scoping `artha.bhavcopy.startup-catchup=false` to "tests that manipulate bhavcopy tables" — **the failing test CARRIES that property and deadlocked anyway**, because Spring CACHES test contexts: exactly 1 of the module's 67 IT classes sets it, every other cached context fires the catch-up's fire-and-forget write at boot, and on the 2-core runner a neighbour context is still mid-batch-upsert into `nse_eod_bhavcopy` when any test's whole-table DELETE runs. Hazard = ANY cached-context writer vs ANY test. Fix: the default moves onto `MarketDataIntegrationTestBase`'s `@DynamicPropertySource` (all 67 contexts; outranks `@SpringBootTest` properties, so re-enabling needs deliberate friction), javadoc re-scoped to the true story, and a pin test in a context that does NOT set the property (red-proofed: removing the default flips it to FAILURE). DELIBERATELY no sort-before-upsert — production is single-writer by construction (single-thread executor, D10, no full-table DELETE outside tests); speculative lock-ordering on an unreachable path declined per working principle 2. | **DONE — [#1141](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1141) @ `920a4305`, MERGED 2026-07-31.** 3 review rounds; the review closed a SECOND door the fix had left open — the 19:30-IST eod-cron is 14:00 UTC (prime CI hours), the identical race through the scheduled writer; the substrate now disables BOTH (`startup-catchup=false` + `eod-cron=-`), pinned by a two-door test in a property-silent context, red-proofed. The review also FALSIFIED the fix's own documented opt-out (subclass `@DynamicPropertySource` runs BEFORE the base's, so the base always wins — everything fails silently toward disabled; both docs now say there is NO in-hierarchy opt-out). Test-code + javadoc only; zero production behaviour change — production stays safe by construction (one AtomicBoolean + single-thread executor across startup/cron/manual/refetch, no whole-table DELETE outside tests), confirmed independently by the review. |

| task_6903cd5e | 2026-07-31 | **A FAILED corporate-action event is UNRESUMABLE by construction — the job recovers from a crash but not from an error, even though the error leaves the symbol strictly WORSE off.** `CorporateActionJob.sweepSymbol` resumes only when the latest event status is `BASE_REBUILT`. Its own javadoc calls that checkpoint "the SOLE resume trigger", because after a rebuild the cache equals Kite so detection yields no divergence and no fresh `DETECTED` row — reasoning that applies equally to `FAILED`, which is not checked. The sequence is purge → base re-fetch → chunked cagg refresh, so a failure at the refresh step leaves the base rebuilt and all five caggs purged and never rebuilt, with **nothing that will ever retry**. Measured at filing: **13 symbols** stranded at ~1% of a control's cagg rows (ULTRACEMCO 2,850 `candles_5m` vs INFY 211,783), all with FAILED events on/after 2026-07-21; **0 of 27** control symbols affected. Scoping the sweep via `artha.corporate-actions.symbols` does NOT help — it narrows which symbols are examined, not what the examination concludes. The build must distinguish WHERE the failure occurred (a purge/base-fetch failure is NOT safe to resume refresh-only over incomplete base data) and must bound retries so a permanently-failing symbol does not re-alert nightly. Source: `docs/audits/2026-07-31-e2e-orchestrated-findings.md` §13.5. Distinct from [#1151](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1151), which fixes the decompression-limit CAUSE; this is the durable fix for the unresumable STATE that outlives it. Today's 13 are being repaired operationally by flipping their latest event to `BASE_REBUILT` so the existing resume path picks them up | **DONE [#1156](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1156) @ 72fd45c8** — failure-STAGE-aware resumable state (marketdata V051 `ca_refresh_retry_state`: refresh-stage failures resume, purge/base-fetch failures do NOT; bounded `refresh_attempts` + errdetail capture so a permanently-failing symbol cannot re-alert nightly); sweep silent-failure audit at [#1162](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1162) @ 1fac6f1b; deployed 2026-08-01; the 13 repaired operationally same night (`UPDATE 13`) |

| task_3e4bae86 | 2026-08-02 | **Manas exit-stop doctrine — should the Chandelier trail drive intraday exits, or stay EOD-managed?** Split out of PR #1221 (M40) after its cross-vendor review correctly found the 6% aggregate risk figure reads stops that never ratchet, and its suggested remedy (persist the ratcheted trail into `paper_positions.stop_loss`) was implemented then caught in Architect audit. Decide the doctrine on measurement, not on doctrine text; also enumerate every consumer of the column and state whether any is served wrongly by the stale initial stop. | **STAY EOD-MANAGED — do NOT persist the trail, and do not add a second column yet. Confidence HIGH on the recommendation, LOW on the P&L behind it. DONE (measurement + docs only, zero code) — `docs/signal-analysis/2026-08-02-manas-exit-stop-doctrine.md`.** Three reasons. (1) **The intraday path is structurally non-functional, which the fork as posed did not capture:** `ticks:last` carries 181 fields, all indices and index derivatives, and ZERO equities, so `PaperBracketEvaluator` iterates all 6 open manas rows every 15s, reads `stop_loss`, finds no tick and skips — every pass since the book opened. All 7 automated closes are at ~20:05 IST (the EOD batch); NONE came from the poll. So the discarded M40 attempt would have been INERT today, not an immediate force-exit — worse, since it would have armed itself silently the day an equity got subscribed. (2) **Underpowered:** full-intraday counterfactual over the book's whole life gives +₹1,141 on 9 of 14 differing legs, but t=+0.88 (needs 2.31), sign-test p=0.090, sign FLIPS on dropping 3 of 9, and 100bp slippage leaves +₹40. The narrower trail-only variant looks better (5 of 5 legs positive, +₹1,977) until decomposed: 63% of it is 3 touched-but-recovered legs that all happened to roll over afterwards (a 3/3 coin, p=0.125), and the other 37% goes to ₹0 under a pessimistic fill that no 1m data exists to refute. Settling it needs ~30 touch events (~9-10 months at 3 per 4 weeks) PLUS intraday bars that only exist if the equities are subscribed — i.e. the change itself. (3) **Residual:** the column is correct for EXITS and only its NAME misleads, but the brief's expected "nothing else reads it" is not quite right — `PaperEmissionGuard.openRiskInr:80-91` feeding the 6% pyramid cap (`ManasPyramidPolicy:100`) DOES read it and IS served wrongly, charging winners full initial risk forever and over-refusing adds; its own test `PaperEmissionGuardTest:34` documents a "trailed ABOVE entry" case whose production branch is dead. Right fix is M40's in-memory calc at the point of use, not a column change. **Premise corrections:** id=28 is CLOSED (07-17 MANUAL) not an open SELL, and carried a NULL stop; `eod-managed-books` is referenced ONCE (`PaperStaleTickAlerter:118`) and suppresses a starvation ALERT, not an exit — tick starvation is the real mechanism; PR #1221 is still OPEN so no in-memory calc is on main. **Harness validated** (reproduces 7/7 automated exits date+reason; cap-bound stops match to the paisa off the entry-bar CLOSE, which is the engine's entry basis — a wrong basis had hidden one arming). **Side-finding chipped separately:** 2026-07-17 was a trading session with ZERO swing equity evaluation and OMAXAUTO closed below its stop unevaluated — an EOD availability gap whose correct remedy is the existing batch-liveness/catch-up machinery, NOT an exit-doctrine change. |

  ([#582](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/582), 2026-07-05):** `BScholesMerton`
  in `black76-math` — spot+dividend-yield q pricing (price/IV reuse Black-76 on F=S·e^((r-q)T);
  spot greeks are the distinct BSM closed forms), FD-verified. DORMANT — index options keep Black-76,
  and a live equity-option wire-in additionally needs a dividend-yield (q) data source we don't have.
  **~~20-level depth flattening~~ SOURCE-BLOCKED (2026-07-05 recon) — do NOT build dead code:** the
  Kite/Upstox retail feeds only disseminate **5** levels (their wire DTOs hold what the feed sends);
  true 20-level needs NSE's separate paid depth feed we don't subscribe to. Plus depth is dropped at
  the domain `Quote` boundary with ZERO consumer and is live-only (mock can't test it). Nothing to
  flatten to 20, no reader — DEFER until the 20-depth feed is subscribed.
- ~~Native 3-min option-snapshot capture~~ **MOOT (2026-07-04 empirical check) — do not re-flag:** the
  backlog premise (5-min capture, want 3-min for Table-2 fidelity) is stale. Live capture already runs
  at ~1-minute granularity (verified in `options_chain_snapshots`), FINER than 3-min; any coarser
  interval (incl. 3-min) is a read-time rollup off the 1-min base. Fidelity goal already exceeded.

    ([#613](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/613)).

  `c0d36310`; owner "deploy chandelier + disarm pyramid on both live and back"): the canonical Chandelier
  (highest-high − 2×rolling-ATR(20), +9% arm, breakeven floor, close exit) is now the operative live-paper
  Manas exit in BOTH engines — doctrine-equivalent, not byte-identical (double[] vs BigDecimal). Pyramiding
  **DISARMED** — live `ARTHA_MANAS_ARORA_PYRAMID_ENABLED=false` (it degrades Sharpe 0.96→0.61 under the tight
  Chandelier trail), backtest headline flipped `rs-turnover-pyramid → rs-turnover-nopyramid` (pyramid kept as
  a labeled A/B probe). Live-verified (sha==HEAD, pyramid off in-container, Manas re-published). Doc:
  `docs/strategies/manas-h4-chandelier-backtest-2026-07-07.md`.
- **OPEN (owner "add to pending, take it later") — FIFO vs RS-priority slot admission for the RS-gated funnel.**
  The H4 compare's headline "45 vs 24" was FIFO-**gross** vs RS-priority-**net** (confounded). Like-for-like on
  `rs-turnover` (our live config, H4 run 2026-07-07): FIFO-gross **45.0** > RS-priority-gross **32.1** at equal
  DD (~50%) and Sharpe (~0.96), turnover ~equal (1270 vs 1322 trades) → inferred **FIFO-net ~34–37% >
  RS-priority-net 23.8%**. RS-priority IS a real edge (it crushes FIFO in the non-gated `technical`/`turnover`
  variants, 53 vs 30 gross) but is **redundant with the funnel's RS-rank gate** (F1) — re-sorting freed slots
  by RS just concentrates into the top-RS names for no extra selection edge. **To decide (both cheap,
  reversible, no live change):** (a) add a `portfolioFifoNet` accounting to `ManasAroraBacktestService` +
  re-run → a direct net-vs-net read (clean, analytics-only, outside the parity firewall; the FIFO-net figure
  above is inferred, not measured); (b) measure how often the live 20:05 batch actually exceeds the 7-slot
  cap (if rare, the whole question is low-impact live). If confirmed, move live admission to FIFO within the
  gated pool (or raise `max_positions`) — a HOLD-tier live-admission change. Caveat: literal "FIFO" doesn't
  map to a same-day batch (entries fire together at EOD), so the real live lever is "don't pile into top-RS on
  an over-subscribed day / raise the cap."
- **No buildable code left otherwise;** the one open perf follow-up is the audit LOW "serial/N+1 backtest
  reads" (`ManasAroraBacktestService.readSeries`).

  400 against the rankable-metric allowlist (source of truth: backtest `MetricsCalculator` + `BacktestRunner`, + `oos_fold_mean`)
  instead of NaN-ing every trial. No new route/param/response-code (400s are raised, not declared) → api-surface gate
  unchanged. +11 tests, 87 pass, ruff clean; ci-optimizer green; admin-merged past the two documented 2-core e2e flakes
  (Python-only → cannot touch the FE WS/signals e2e). **Follow-up [`#651`](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/651)
  (post-deploy live-verify caught it):** #648 ran the validation AFTER `resolve()`, whose `raise_for_status()` on a bad
  `strategyId` leaked a **500** before the numeric checks — moved the request-only knobs (objective/walkForward/maxTrials/
  seed/earlyStopping) ahead of the resolve round-trip so bad input is a fast 400 regardless of strategy validity (+1 test,
  88 pass, all CI incl. e2e green). **DEPLOYED + LIVE-VERIFIED** in-container: `maxTrials:"lots"` → `400 "maxTrials must be
  an integer"`; `objective.metric:"shrape"` → `400 "unknown objective metric 'shrape'; expected one of [...]"`.
- **M20 swing sell-decision FE surface — SHIPPED** ([`#649`](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/649), CI in flight):
  the daily Minervini + Manas Arora sell-decision triad was curl-only. New `/strategies/swing-sell-decisions` page —
  `api/swing.ts` over the two read-only `/sell-decisions` endpoints (recompute on read), a book toggle + per-holding table
  (setup, entry/current, signed unrealized %, base stop, current trail, "buyable now?" chip, HOLD/SELL verdict badge),
  route + MegaMenu entry. Pure consumption of existing typed endpoints; no backend change. Verify trio green (lint/build
  clean, 261 tests, coverage 51.09%). **DEPLOYED + LIVE-VERIFIED**: frontend-react rebuilt + recreated healthy; the new
  `/strategies/swing-sell-decisions` route is present in the served bundle.
- **Remaining = owner/data-gated only** (option 3): the live-paper reliability month, an always-on host, the flag-flips
  (F9 paper-risk arm / F7 promotion / Dow-factor), and the owner-call audit HIGH/MED doctrine items (H6/H8, the swing
  exit-parity HOLD batch, register accepted single-owner tradeoffs). Nothing further is clean-autonomous without opening
  one of those gates.

### 8f. F9/F7 arm-effect audit + config fix (2026-07-10)
Owner asked "what effect will F9/F7/audit-doctrine have on the live app" → verified against the live code (not memory):
- **F9 (`ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_ENABLED`) arm = a narrow SCALPER circuit-breaker, no number moves.** The flag gates ONE thing —
  the heat-cap BLOCK in `RiskService.entryAllowed` (`RiskService.java:146-156`): on the scalper book only (its `heat_cap_pct`
  is the sole seed with `enabled=true`, V023; swing rows inert), when open SPAN margin ≥60% of book equity, NEW paper entries
  stop being opened + a `risk_audit` TRIP row + 1 ntfy. Never resizes, never touches an open position, changes ZERO displayed
  figures (`advised_lots`/`margin_snapshot` are already stamped, advisory, not even surfaced in the FE). Adds a synchronous
  margin HTTP hop to the scalper entry path. The 10%→3% scalper daily-loss tighten is already live, flag-independent.
- **F7 (`ARTHA_GRADUATION_PROMOTION_ENABLED`) arm = effectively NO live effect.** Armed, the 21:00-IST evaluator only writes a
  `strategy_graduations` MARKER row + 1 push when a strategy clears ≥50 closed paper trades / expectancy>0 / Sharpe≥0.5 / DD≤25%.
  It does NOT republish, NOT flip `published_version_id`/`status`, NOT swap any live config, NOT change what trades — the V024
  migration header pins "NEVER arms … the owner decides any real live change", and no signal/paper code reads the marker table.
  No FE consumes `/graduation/promotions`. Worst case of arming = a spurious marker + a phone alert. (Task title "shadow→champion"
  is a misnomer — it promotes nothing.)
- **Audit HIGH/MED doctrine = code changes, not flags; the only lever that moves owner numbers** → correctly held for review:
  H6 (CA-unadjusted screener → candidate lists shift near corporate actions), H8 (cheat-3c mislabel → which setups fire), the
  swing exit-parity batch #128 / M36–M40 (exit timing+price → swing paper P&L). M1 matters only once F9 armed; M17/M18/M28/M31
  = no owner-facing behavior change.
- **Config bug fixed in passing** ([`#653`](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/653)): `application.yml:100` read
  env `ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_PER_TRADE_PCT` but compose/.env pass `ARTHA_PAPER_RISK_PER_TRADE_RISK_PCT` (with `RISK`) — so the
  per-trade sizing knob was non-wireable from `.env` (silently fell back to the 1.0 default). Aligned the placeholder to the
  code-bound property `artha.paper.risk.per-trade-risk-pct` (`PaperService.java:143`) + the compose/.env name. Advisory-only
  (advised_lots), so no behaviour change at the default; the knob is now actually settable. Config-only, packages clean.

| **05** | **Risk governor — 2 interface leaks** | WORTH EXPLORING | `RiskService`, `PaperService.settle()` re-poll | **PARTLY DONE 2026-07-28 — the CAS leak is FIXED; the null-price overload is DECLINED with reasons.** §8a M1 prerequisite confirmed DONE (#739, deployed 2026-07-12), so this was unblocked. **Real defect found:** `settle()` returned the realized amount whether or not it won the compare-and-swap close, so a caller could not tell "I closed it" from "someone else already had". **SIX paths acted on it** — I found three, cross-vendor review found the other three, and the ones I missed were the worse half: (1) the 15s bracket sweep counted the position, logged `"paper STOP_LOSS closed position N at <ltp>"` and cleared its starvation flag for an exit performed by someone else at a price that was not the settlement price; (2) `settleExpiries` and (3) `settlePastExpiries` inflated their `settled` counts, feeding `PaperScheduler`'s operator-facing totals; (4) **`closePosition` returned the WINNER's trade on a lost race, so `PaperController` wrote a `MANUAL_CLOSE` audit row for a close the request never performed — attributing someone else's exit to a human operator**; (5) `closeForSignal` and (6) `markToCloseIntraday` counted unconditionally, feeding `EngineExitListener`, `PaperScheduler` and `TelegramCommandBot`. A seventh site was the same class: `staleSettleUsed` fired BEFORE the CAS, so a race loser emitted "settled off a stale tick" having settled nothing. `closeForPosition` is deliberately UNCHANGED — its desired-state contract must read already-closed as success or effects retry forever. Nothing was mis-BOOKED (the CAS itself was always right — no duplicate fill, no double event); the damage was to the RECORD. `settle`/`settleExpiry` now return `Optional`, empty on a lost race. The old return value was consumed nowhere, so the signature change carried no behavioural risk. **DECLINED, deliberately:** the row's other half — make `settle` take an explicit settlement price to kill the null-means-LTP overload — would push the #694 close-path doctrine (last REAL tick at ANY age; refuse only when never ticked) into three call sites. Centralising that rule is exactly what keeps it correct in one place, so the documented overload stays. |
| ~~**06**~~ | ~~**Session-rolled reader** — two 1m→N rollups on different grids collapsed into one~~ **INVESTIGATED + DECLINED 2026-07-29 — DO NOT RE-FLAG.** The two anchors are not drift; each is load-bearing and they are mutually exclusive. `ConnectingDotsService.bucketStart` floors from IST **midnight** for **pg `time_bucket` parity** (its own comment says so) — a read-time rollup must agree with DB-side bucketing or it disagrees with the caggs. `FuturesOiChartService.bucketStart` floors from **09:15 SESSION_OPEN** so the OI series left-joins onto the candle grid it shares (its javadoc states both sides use the SAME function precisely for that). Collapsing satisfies one and breaks the other. Measured: 09:15 = **555 min** past midnight, so the grids are **IDENTICAL for 1/3/5/15m** (555 mod N = 0) and **DIVERGE for 10/30/60m** (remainders 5/15/15) — both services support all seven `OiInterval` values, so the divergence is live on three of them. The correct deliverable was this documentation, not a refactor. | WORTH EXPLORING → **DECLINED** | `ConnectingDotsService.resample` (midnight-IST grid) vs `FuturesOiChartService.aggregate/bucketStart` (session-open grid) | **DECLINED 2026-07-29 — closed, do not re-flag** (the verdict is in the title cell; this cell keeps the reasoning history). ⚠️ **I marked this MOOT on 2026-07-28 and was WRONG — cross-vendor review caught it with file:line evidence.** The reasoning error is worth keeping: I grepped for the SQL fold shape (`INTERVAL '" + minutes`) under `candles/` only, found exactly one (`CandleRepository.rangeRolledFromOneMinute`), and concluded there was one rollup in the whole service. The other two are **Java-side** folds in different packages — `ConnectingDotsService.java:469-504` buckets 1m candles from **midnight IST**, `FuturesOiChartService.java:154-176` from the **09:15 session open**, and BOTH serve 60m (`OiInterval`), so a 09:47 bar lands on 09:00 in one and 09:15 in the other. That is precisely the two-grids divergence this row names. [#1033](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1033) fixed the SIM-vs-LIVE 1h anchor in strategy-engine; it never touched these. Searching for one implementation shape and concluding about all implementations is the error — grep the CONCEPT (any 1m→N fold), not one spelling of it. **Ride-along** whenever charts are next touched; still not worth a standalone PR. |

### 9b. Secondary friction (fold into the above or defer)
- ~~**`PaperService.settle()` leaky tx boundary**~~ **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — all three halves resolved, and the remaining one was NOT a defect. DO NOT RE-FLAG.** (a) CAS-close-first: **FIXED** 2026-07-28 (row 05). (b) null-price-means-LTP: **DECLINED** with reasons (it would push the #694 last-real-tick doctrine into three call sites; centralising it is what keeps it correct). (c) `@Transactional`-only-because-one-caller-isn't: **investigated across all 10 call sites — the boundary is CORRECT everywhere.** The proposed fix ("let the close path own its tx") is what the code already does. ⚠️ The real risk here is not the annotation but **self-invocation bypassing Spring's proxy** — the same trap that silently dropped a tx in `RegistryService` (PF-01 round-6 #2) — and all four self-invoking callers (`closePosition`, `closeForSignal`, `closeForPosition`, `markToCloseIntraday`) are each independently `@Transactional`, while both external callers (`PaperBracketEvaluator`, `PaperExpiryService`) go through the proxy. **What WAS wrong was the javadoc guarding the invariant**: it listed three self-invoking callers when there were four, and called `PaperBracketEvaluator` "the ONE external caller" after `PaperExpiryService` became a second. Rewritten to state the RULE (external ⇒ nothing to do; self-invoking ⇒ that method MUST carry its own `@Transactional`) instead of a caller list that drifts. This matters because the failure is SILENT — the close succeeds and only its AFTER_COMMIT listeners, the TAKEN-anchor resolver and auto-journal, quietly do not fire. ⚠️ **The "take an explicit settlement price" half is DECLINED (2026-07-28, see row 05)** — it would push the #694 last-real-tick doctrine into three call sites; centralising it is what keeps it correct.
- **`ExitEvaluator` index-convention leak** (`ExitEvaluator.java:288–306`) — **INVESTIGATED 2026-07-30: no defect found. NO code change made — one was written, reviewed, and REVERTED.** Callers each reconstruct `entryIndex` and pick between two `evaluate` overloads with parity-safety as tribal knowledge. Findings:
  - **Overload hazard is prospective, not actual.** All 7 production call sites already pick correctly — `TickwiseGoldenRunner` 223/299/352 take the 5-arg (tiers ACTIVE); `SignalEngine` 1457/1903, `SwingBatchEngine` 804, `SwingSellDecisionService` 175 take the 4-arg (live, no tiers). The 4-arg javadoc warns a live `scaled_exit` would be “mishandled as a full close”; nothing enforces it, so this is a latent trap for a NEW caller, not a live bug.
  - **The two not-found policies diverge and that is FINE — the divergence is load-bearing.** `SwingBatchEngine:791` SKIPS on `entryIndex < 0`; `SwingSellDecisionService:161` CLAMPS with `Math.max(entryIndex, 0)` for the two advisory levels while its sell verdict abstains via its own `entryIndex >= 0` guard. **⚠️ The clamp is doctrine-asymmetric, and that asymmetry is the real finding of this investigation:**
    - MANAS — stop is `basis: atr_multiple`, so `entryLevels` resolves `ATR.valueAt(0)` = **null** (`Ta4jIndicators.wrap` returns null below `unstable = period`, and `requirePositive` forces `period >= 1`, so this is structural, not a fixture artifact). `ManasDoctrine.trailLevel` carries its own `entryIndex >= 0` guard. Both levels null.
    - MINERVINI — stop is `basis: percent, value: 8`, which reads only `position.entryPrice()` and **never the series**, and `MinerviniDoctrine.trailLevel` ignores `position`/`entryIndex` entirely (sma50 at `lastIndex`). Both levels are **VALID numbers**.
  - **What I got wrong, and what caught it.** I wrote a “hardening” that made both levels null-by-construction when `entryIndex < 0`, on the theory that the clamp emitted null anyway. That theory holds only for the index-DEPENDENT basis; run backwards it is false, because the index-INDEPENDENT bases return valid values. The change would have silently suppressed Minervini's 8% stop and 50-day trail. My red-proof and my test were both Manas-only — the one doctrine where null was already correct — so both came back green and I read that as proof of byte-identity. **Cross-vendor review (gpt-5.6-sol, xhigh) caught it as a Major.** Reverted; the tree carries no behaviour change.
  - **Reachability, corrected.** An earlier draft of this row said the window was 180 days, read from `LiveSeriesStore` — the WRONG code path (that is the live engine's series). `SwingSellDecisionService` fetches `doctrine.warmupDays()`, **520** by default for both doctrines. Oldest open anchor is ~28 days. Note `activeEntries()` applies no maximum anchor-age filter, so “unreachable” is a statement about today's book, not an invariant.
  - **Kept:** a CHARACTERIZATION test pinning the Manas semantics, whose javadoc states the Minervini path differs and why. **NOT done:** `IndicatorBank.positionAt(timestamp)` (the one-derivation fix) — still the right shape if a fifth caller appears, unjustified at zero defects. **Parity-adjacent — HOLD-tier**; row stays OPEN.
  - ~~**Coverage gap found:** no test of `SwingSellDecisionService` under the Minervini doctrine.~~ **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — [#1130](https://github.com/prashantm912/artha-yantra-2/pull/1130) @ `6763b95a`** (chip `task_de025c5f`). `MinerviniSellDecisionServiceTest`: the normal holding (stop = entry × 0.92, trail = sma50 at the last bar), the entry-bar-outside-window case, and a SELL on the stop breach. **Red-proofed against the exact reverted change** — with it reapplied, `ManasAroraSellDecisionServiceTest` passes 2/2 while the new test fails on `keepsBothAdvisoryLevelsWhenTheEntryBarPredatesTheWindow`, IN THE SAME RUN. That pair is the artifact: it makes the blindness visible rather than described. ⚠️ Fixture traps, both of which bit the first cut: a FLAT series is wrong for the HOLD case because close EQUALS sma50 and the MV-7.4 close-below trail fires, so it silently exercises the trail instead; and isolating the 8% stop needs a RISING series against a HIGHER entry (close 136.8 < stop 138 while sma50 ≈131.9 stays BELOW the close) — on a falling series sma50 sits above the close throughout, both rules fire, and the cause is ambiguous. Reviewer confirmed the pre-window timestamp genuinely resolves `indexAtOrBefore` to −1, the detail sits in the `minerviniDetail` slot (not an adjacent one), and production SMA warmup is `period − 1` so the test's independent 50-close mean is a valid oracle.
- **Triplicated index-name canonicalization** (speculative) — same index identity in 3 drifting maps (`UnderlyingRef`/`OpenAlgoExchange`/`expiredUnderlying`), disagreeing coverage → one bidirectional `IndexRef`. (Leave the per-broker symbol/expiry mappers — ADR-0001/0002.)
- **`SweepService` repo-factory dance ×8** (speculative) — the per-thread `jobs=factory(); try/finally close()` lifecycle inlined in 8 methods → a `with self._unit_of_work()` context manager. Plus the OI-reader 6× fallback copy + 3× scheduler alert wrapper (both fold into 01/02).
